and `_core.core_actions()` (`CORE_ACTIONS` with the docstrings: `(name, doc, accepts_executor_keys, [(sub, function, doc,
accepts_executor_keys)])`). They run on the façade session (`facade::worker_session`), print through `sys.stdout` / `sys.stderr`
(the GIL is taken per write, so `capsys` and redirected streams see the output), read a snapshot of the live Python config and
load the plugin manifest before dispatching (as `mrsm` does). `context` carries what the config registry does not know: the
`_core.SqlConnector`s of SQL connectors this process defined ad hoc and the attributes of every ad hoc connector (`api:` /
`valkey:` are built natively, every key is told to the Python workers).

| Surface | v4.1.4 | v5 | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `meerschaum.actions.actions[name]` | the v4 function of `meerschaum/actions/<name>.py` | a façade `f(action=None, **kw)` with v4's `__name__` (`sync`, `_sync_pipes` for subactions), docstring and signature (`executor_keys` where v4 had it), which calls `_core.run_action([name, *action], kw, context)`; plugin actions (`@make_action`) are still the plugins' functions | one implementation |
| `get_subactions`, `get_action`, `get_main_action_name`, `choose_subaction`, `choices_docstring` | module introspection | the same code; a core action's subactions come from the native registry, plugin actions keep module introspection | plugin SDK |
| `get_completer(core action)` | the module's `complete_<name>` | `None`: the native shell completes the standard actions | |
| `meerschaum.actions.<module>` (`sync`, `show`, ...), `api.routes.*`, `api._events` / `_websockets` / `_chunks`, `api.dash.{callbacks,pages,jobs,plugins,tokens,users,webterm,websockets,sync}`, `api.dash.assets`, `_internal.term`, `_internal.cli`, `_internal.shell.{ShellCompleter,ValidAutoSuggest,updates}` | importable | `ModuleNotFoundError` naming the replacement (`_internal/_removed.py`, a finder at the end of `sys.meta_path`) | spec §4: removed modules fail with a message |
| `meerschaum.api.dash` | built the v4 console and mounted it on FastAPI | a light module: `make_dash_app` / `get_dash_app` (delegating to `api/host/dash.py`), `dash_app = None`, `debug`, `endpoints`, `get_api_connector`; `sessions`, `components`, `keys`, `pipes`, `graphs`, `connectors` stay for plugin-owned Dash apps (the console's offcanvas, console div and `build_pages_offcanvas_children` are gone) | §8.4 |
| `meerschaum.api` | built the app, imported routes, events, websockets, the console | the app and helpers only (`app`, `get_api_connector`, `get_pipe`, `pipes`, `manager`, `ScopedAuth`, ...); `meerschaum.api.host` serves what only Python can | §7.3 |
| `meerschaum.entry(sysargs, _patch_args=None, _use_cli_daemon=True, _session_id=None)` | parsed the arguments in Python, optionally in a warm CLI daemon (`experimental:cli_daemon`) | `_core.entry`; `_use_cli_daemon` and `_session_id` are ignored (there is no CLI daemon) | |
| `entry_without_daemon`, `entry_with_args` | the Python dispatcher | the same names, delegating to `_core.entry` / `_core.run_action` (`entry_with_args(action=[...], **kw)` takes the full action words, as v4) | |
| `_internal.entry._do_action_wrapper` | the action runner | unchanged: the plugin worker runs plugin actions with it (venv activation, `filter_keywords`, traceback logging) | worker |
| `get_shell(...)` / `Shell(...).cmdloop()` | the `cmd.Cmd` shell with `prompt_toolkit` | an object whose `cmdloop()` runs the native `mrsm` shell as a child process on the terminal (`onecmd(line)` runs one command natively). The SDK hooks (`_insert_shell_actions`, `_remove_shell_actions`, `revert_input`, `shell_attrs`, `get_shell_intro`, `entry._shells`) are gone together with their call sites in `plugins`, `utils/packages` and `Daemon` | the shell is native |
| `python -m meerschaum`, the `meerschaum` console script | the Python CLI | `execv` of the `mrsm` binary from `meerschaum-cli` (`_internal/binary.py`: `$MRSM_BIN`, the interpreter's scripts directory, `$PATH`, the newest `target/{release,debug}/mrsm` of a source checkout; a Python script named `mrsm` is skipped so the shim cannot start itself); `MRSM_PYTHON` defaults to the running interpreter so workers use the same environment; a missing binary exits 127 naming `pip install meerschaum-cli` / `MRSM_BIN` | D8 | (superseded in wave 17: see "Wave 17 (task W)" below: the extension runs the CLI in-process; `MRSM_BIN` still execs a binary)
| `_internal.arguments._parser.parse_help`, `parse_version` | printed help / version in Python | removed (the binary does); the argparse parser itself stays: plugin arguments (`add_plugin_argument`), the worker's `parse_arguments` for plugin actions and the manifest's argument groups | worker, SDK |
| Ctrl-C during `mrsm.entry(...)` / `actions[...]` | `KeyboardInterrupt` at the next bytecode | the dispatcher runs on its own thread while the interpreter's thread polls `check_signals()` every 50 ms: the first Ctrl-C sets the stop flag that looping actions (`--loop`, schedules, `start pipeline`) poll and the call raises `KeyboardInterrupt` once the action has wound down; a second Ctrl-C, or an action still running 10 s after the first, raises at once (the thread finishes at its next checkpoint). The extension installs **no** process-wide SIGINT / SIGTERM handler (`native::jobs::set_embedded(true)` at import turns `install_stop_handler` into a no-op: the handler, which ends the process on a second signal, belongs to the `mrsm` binary), so an earlier entry can no longer make a later Ctrl-C kill the interpreter | tests `test_sigint_after_a_native_entry_leaves_the_interpreter_alone`, `test_sigint_stops_a_looping_entry_and_raises_keyboard_interrupt` |

## Wave 16 (Agent Q): scope fixes (intentional v4 changes)

| Id | v4 behavior | v5 behavior | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| W16Q-001 | `POST /jobs/{name}/stop` requires `["jobs:execute", "josb:stop"]` (`api/routes/_jobs.py:213`); `josb:stop` is a typo of `jobs:stop`, so only `*` tokens and administrators could stop jobs | requires `["jobs:execute", "jobs:stop"]` (`routes/jobs.rs::stop_job`); a token that explicitly holds the legacy `josb:stop` is accepted in place of `jobs:stop` (`auth::scoped_auth_with_aliases`); the 403 names `jobs:stop`. The old `// PARITY` comment is gone | user-requested fix; the typo made the route unusable for scoped tokens. Tests: `routes.rs::stopping_a_job_needs_jobs_stop_and_still_accepts_the_legacy_spelling` (a non-admin token also needs `actions:execute`, which the non-admin actions gate asks of everyone) |
| W16Q-002 | `edit tokens` (`Scope (`*` to grant all permissions):`) and `edit users` replace a typed `*` with `STATIC_CONFIG['tokens']['scopes']`, so a "grant all" token lacked custom plugin scopes and scopes added later | `*` is stored literally (`native/tokens.rs`, `native/users.rs`); the prompts read "`*` grants all scopes, including custom and future scopes"; the console's token register and edit forms have 4.1.6's "Grant all scopes" switch (on by default and for a token holding `*`; off lists the built-in scopes and a custom-scopes field; `token_payload` in `meerschaum-web/src/pages.rs` stores `["*"]` literally and refuses an empty selection with "Select at least one scope."). The forms are structured fields, not the JSON textarea of the earlier draft. `register tokens --scopes '*'`, `POST /tokens/register` and the Python `meerschaum.core.Token` already kept a literal `*` (no façade change). A token or user created with **no** scopes still gets the built-in list (v4 default, kept so recorded fixtures hold); scope names are never validated | user-requested; the checks (`auth.rs` `granted.has("*")`, `_oauth2.py`, MCP `has_scope`) already honored `*`. Tests: `cli/tests/residual_actions_a.rs::a_star_scope_is_stored_literally_for_tokens_and_users`, `web/tests/console.rs::the_token_form_grants_all_scopes_with_a_star_kept_literally`, `routes.rs::a_star_scope_is_stored_literally_and_grants_every_scope`, `tests/api_host/test_star_scope.py` |
| W16Q-003 | `is_user_allowed_to_execute` compares the necessary scopes (`actions:execute`, `jobs:execute`) by membership, so a non-admin holding `*` was refused ("Missing scope 'actions:execute'.") | a literal `*` satisfies them (`auth.rs`, before the loop over `NECESSARY_SCOPES`) | a `*` holder must be able to run actions and jobs; follows from W16Q-002 |


## Wave 16 (task P1): the Python core and the `MRSM_CORE` gate are deleted (D14)

`meerschaum/_core/_gate.py`, `GATED_FEATURES` / `GATED_CLASSES`, every `rust_impl()` / `rust_method()` guard and every
`MRSM_CORE` read are gone: the façade calls `_core` directly. `import meerschaum` raises `CoreNotBuiltError` (an
`ImportError` naming `scripts/rust/build_py.sh` and the wheel) when `meerschaum/_core/_native.abi3.so` is missing; the
extension itself is still loaded lazily (first `_core.<name>` read), so a bare `import meerschaum` stays at 119 ms and
loads nothing but the standard library. Deleted in `meerschaum/{connectors/sql,core,utils,config,_core}`: about 16,900
lines of Python (the bodies of every guarded function, the SQL query builders behind them, the Python partition,
compression, maintenance and temporary-table code, the polars `filter_existing` fallback, the Python sync / verify /
fetch / dedup / get_data bodies, the PyYAML / `ruamel.yaml` fallback of the config codec).

| Module | Now |
|---|---|
| `connectors/sql/{_pipes,_users,_plugins,_compress,_maintenance,_partition,_fetch,_uri}.py` | `return self._rust_method('<name>')(...)` delegations with the v4 signatures and docstrings (1,864 lines left of 8,293, mostly docstrings). `_instance.py` (temporary-table bookkeeping) is deleted. `_sql.py`, `_create_engine.py`, `tables/`, `_cli.py` stay (D16) |
| `connectors/sql/_SQLConnector.py` | `_core_connector()` builds the `_core.SqlConnector` once per process and raises `RuntimeError` when no driver can be built; `_rust_method(name)` returns the bound method (`None` for names the extension does not define) |
| `core/Pipe/{_sync,_data,_fetch,_dtypes,_deduplicate,_verify}.py` | one `_core.pipe_*` call per public method; `_rust_context` (`required=`) builds the call context. `SyncResult` and `_native_context` stay |
| `core/Pipe/{__init__,_attributes}.py` | the `_core.Pipe` is always the parameter store (no Python-mode branch); `get_parameters` is `_core.get_parameters` plus the memo |
| `utils/{dataframe,dtypes,pipes}.py`, `utils/dtypes/sql.py`, `config/*` | the guarded functions are one-line `_core.<name>(...)` calls; the unguarded SDK helpers stay |
| `utils/sql.py` | `sql_item_name` and `build_where` delegate; every other documented helper keeps its v4 implementation (the documented-name baseline lists `get_update_queries`, `get_create_table_queries`, ... under `meerschaum.utils.sql`) |

New bindings for the delegations (`src/sql_api.rs`): `SqlConnector.set_integer_now_func(pipe)`; `SqlConnector.get_pipe_data`
now takes `begin_add_minutes`, `end_add_minutes`, `replace_nulls` and `skip_existing_cols_check` (the keywords of the v4
Python path, which `SQLConnector.get_pipe_data` forwards).

| Id | v4 / parity-phase behavior | v5 behavior | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| W16P1-001 | `SQLConnector.get_add_columns_queries`, `get_alter_columns_queries`, `get_to_sql_dtype`, `create_pipe_table_from_df` | removed (listed under "Removed in 5.0" of the migration guide) | only the Python sync bodies called them; the Rust core builds the same DDL inside `sync_pipe` (golden-tested against v4 in `meerschaum-connector-sql`) |
| W16P1-002 | the guarded functions accepted stub objects as `self` (duck typing) | they need a real connector | the delegation asks the object for its `_core.SqlConnector` |
| W16P1-003 | W15L-006: an `oracle` instance without `libclntsh` fell back to the Python (SQLAlchemy) body with a warning | `RuntimeError`: "no usable Rust driver (the Oracle client library (libclntsh, e.g. Oracle Instant Client) is not installed ...)"; `Pipe.sync()` / `verify()` return it as a failed `SuccessTuple`, the other `Pipe` methods raise it | one implementation |
| W16P1-004 | an instance connector that cannot be parsed or built (`instance='sql:nope'`): the parity-phase `unbuildable` kind ran the v4 body | v4.1.4 behavior, checked against `.venv-ref`: every call that needs the instance (`get_data`, `get_rowcount`, `get_sync_time`, `get_backtrack_data`, `infer_dtypes`, `deduplicate`, `sync`, `verify`, `enforce_dtypes`, `filter_existing`, `exists`) raises `Exception("Unable to parse connector keys 'sql:nope'")`; `fetch()` answers `None` with the `No fetch() function defined` warning when the source has no `fetch()` (v4 checks the source first) | no neutral values: v4 never returned `0`/`None` here (a first draft of this wave did) |
| W16P1-005 | `system:experimental:join_fetch` selected the experimental `_join_fetch_query` | ignored, with a warning once per process | experimental, undocumented, off by default, and never ported to Rust (`metadef.rs` TODO); the rows it skipped are skipped by `filter_existing` anyway |
| W16P1-006 | `meerschaum.utils.yaml` used PyYAML or `ruamel.yaml` when the extension was absent | the native codec only (no package); the wrapper keeps `yaml.load`, `safe_load`, `dump` and `get_dumper_class()` (`None`) | the extension is required |
| W16P1-007 | `need_update()` installed `semver` whenever a package had a version pin (twelve attempts in one sync + `get_data()`), and `is_installed()` / `attempt_import()` replaced a too-old package (a `pandas` below 3) with `pandas[parquet]>=3.0.0` in `venvs/mrsm` | versions are compared by `_core.version_satisfies` (PEP 440); a too-old package counts as installed and `import_pandas()` / `attempt_import(check_update=True)` warn once with the upgrade command; nothing is installed by a version check | the base wheel installs nothing implicitly beyond a missing package, and never a replacement |
| W16P1-008 | `interval_str` imported `humanfriendly` (installed on first use) | in-tree `_format_timespan`, identical to `humanfriendly.format_timespan` on 40,000 sampled values | `humanfriendly` is not a dependency |
| W16P1-009 | without the `sql` extra `exec` / `read` raised `AttributeError: 'NoneType'`, `value` / `.engine` returned `None`, `to_sql` returned `False` | `ImportError` naming `meerschaum[sql]` (and `meerschaum[pandas]` for the frames of `read` / `to_sql`) from `read`, `exec`, `value`, `to_sql`, `exec_queries` and `get_connection`; `.engine` raises an `AttributeError` with the same message (an exception cannot be both, and `hasattr` must stay `False`). `URI`, `DATABASE_URL` and `IS_THREAD_SAFE` are built from the attributes (`get_engine_str`, v4's flavor/database rule) and `table_exists` raises the `ImportError`, so none of them import SQLAlchemy; `Pipe.get_num_workers` sizes the pool from `pool_size` when there is no engine | migration guide: "Raw SQL needs `meerschaum[sql]`" |
| W16P1-010 | `get_pipe_metadef` of a naive `begin` against an aware `end` (or text against a datetime) raised `TypeError` (`begin >= end`) | the façade repeats the comparison before the Rust call: same `TypeError` (integer axes convert first and never raised) | `tests/test_d14_bindings.py` asserts the recorded exception type |
| W16P1-011 | `SQLConnector._rust_method(name)` returned `None` for a name the extension does not bind | `AttributeError` naming the method | a typo in a delegation must fail loudly |

**Tests.** Every test that ran both `MRSM_CORE` modes (`test_native_sync`, `test_pipe_methods_native`, `test_w9_verify`,
`test_w9_get_config`, `test_d14_bindings`, `test_w9_filter_unseen_dtypes`, `crates/meerschaum-py/tests/test_core.py`) keeps its
comparison: the Python-mode half was recorded from the last tree that had the Python core (`tests/oracle/*.json`, type-tagged
JSON read by `tests/_oracle.py`, no `pickle`). These are recordings of the parity-phase Python mode, not v4.1.4 output, and
cannot be re-recorded (the code that produced them is deleted); the tests whose data depends on the pandas major list
themselves in `pandas_dependent` and skip under another major. They are replayed with `oracle.expected()`; tests of the gate, python mode or the
dispatch guards are deleted. Python-internal tests were rewritten against the façade (`test_index_queries.py` runs against a
real PostgreSQL table; `test_filter_existing.py` / `utils/test_dataframe.py` compare Polars input with Pandas input and a row-set
reference) or deleted where `meerschaum-connector-sql` already tests the Rust builder with v4 goldens (`test_normalize_columnstore_cols`,
`test_compress_settings_*`, `test_columnstore_policy_after_scales_on_int_axis`, `test_integer_now_func_registered_for_int_axis`,
`test_partition_ddl_quotes_mixed_case_column`, `test_partition_bounds_deterministic`, `test_get_pipe_data_uses_adbc_for_polars`,
`test_stale_temporary_tables_scan_is_throttled`, `test_compress_with_upsert_warns_once`). The last two have Rust tests in `meerschaum-connector-sql`
(`instance::tables::tests::stale_scan_throttle_window`, `instance_maintenance.rs::test_stale_temporary_tables_scan_is_throttled`,
`::test_compress_with_upsert_warns_once`, `::test_set_integer_now_func`); `test_get_pipe_data_uses_adbc_for_polars` is obsolete
(no ADBC path). The flavor sets the tests used (`VACUUMABLE_FLAVORS`, ...) moved to `tests/connectors.py`.
`bench/bench_boundary.py` and `bench/bench_sync.py` (python vs rust) were removed; `scripts/bench/run.py` (v4.1.4 vs v5) is the
benchmark.

**Still Python, and why.** `core/Pipe/_attributes.py` / `_cache.py` / `_live_parameters.py` keep the attribute and metadata
caches (local TTL, on-disk cache, `LiveDict` views): they hold Python objects that callers mutate, and the Rust side already owns the
parameter store and the resolution. The prompts of `edit`, `bootstrap` and `show`. `config/_default.py` (the default
config the compose `init` and `get_possible_keys` read). `utils/sql.py` and `utils/dtypes/sql.py` helpers that take SQLAlchemy
objects or a connector (D16).

## Wave 16 (task S): `APIConnector`, `ValkeyConnector` (and `meerschaum.jobs`) are façades over Rust

`meerschaum.connectors.api.APIConnector` is a façade over `_core.ApiConnector` (`crates/meerschaum-py/src/api_client_api.rs`, the
`meerschaum_connector_api` client over `reqwest`) and `meerschaum.connectors.valkey.ValkeyConnector` over `_core.ValkeyConnector`
(`src/valkey_api.rs`, `meerschaum_connector_valkey` over `fred`). Each Python connector builds its Rust client lazily, once per process
(`_rust_client()` compares the PID, so a forked job rebuilds its own connections and token); copying and pickling a connector drops the
client. The Valkey client connects on its first command (like `valkey.Valkey`), so building one or reading `URI` never needs a server.
The native flows (`Pipe.sync`, `get_data`, `verify`, the CLI entry) use that same client: `facade::hold_api` / `hold_valkey` let the registry find the Python connector's client by keys and attributes (held weakly, per PID; `_rust_client()` rebuilds the client when the connector's attributes change, so `conn.password = ...` logs in again, as v4 did), so a process logs in once, warns about a failed login once and connects to Valkey once, as v4's one connector object did (`test_native_calls_log_in_once_per_process`, `test_native_calls_warn_about_a_failed_login_once`, `test_native_calls_reuse_the_connectors_connection`). Native calls on a connector the Python process does not hold are built from the config as before.
Neither class imports `requests`, `httpx`, `urllib3`, `redis`, `valkey` or `websockets` (`tests/test_api_connector.py::test_base_path_imports_no_http_client`).
`meerschaum.jobs` / `meerschaum.utils.daemon` are the rows of the jobs section below (`jobs_api.rs`).

The client's warnings (`Failed to get data for ...`, `Failed to log into ...`) reach `meerschaum.utils.warnings.warn(msg, stack=False)` through a
warning sink the extension installs (`install_warning_sink`, `api_client_api.rs`); `login(warn=False)` drops them (`APIConnector::login_with(warn)`; a silent failed login leaves the one-warning-per-connector flag alone, as v4).

**The released 4.1.4 is the oracle.** `tests/test_api_connector.py` runs one battery of about 80 calls (every pipe method with its keyword
arguments, errors included: a missing pipe, unreachable server, wrong credentials, a missing job) and a second one of about 40 for users, plugins and tokens
through the v4 `APIConnector` (`.venv-ref`) and through the façade against the same loopback `mrsm start api`; `tests/test_valkey_connector.py` runs a
battery of about 70 calls (strings, documents, `read`, pipes, users, plugins, `fetch`) through v4's `ValkeyConnector` (with `valkey-py` from the
shared test venv) and the façade against the Valkey container on 6399. After normalizing pipe names, durations and transport error text the results
are equal, with the differences listed below. The key helpers and URIs (`quote_table`, `get_entity_key`, `get_document_key`, `URI`, `from_uri` and its
label rule) are compared with v4 the same way.

| Id | v4.1.4 | v5 | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| W16S-001 | `make_request()` / `get` / `post` / ... return a `requests.Response` | an `ApiResponse` (`_core`) with `status_code`, `ok`, `text`, `content`, `headers` (`Content-Type` only), `url`, `json()`, `raise_for_status()` (`OSError`, as `requests.HTTPError` is) and `bool()` | `requests` is not a dependency (D20). Other `requests.Response` members (`iter_content`, `cookies`, `elapsed`, ...) do not exist. `make_request(**kwargs)` honors `params`, `json`, `data` (text, bytes or a form dict), `files` (one file) and `timeout`; other `requests` options are ignored |
| W16S-002 | a refused or reset connection raises `requests.exceptions.ConnectionError` (an `OSError`) | the builtin `ConnectionError` (an `OSError`) with the transport's message; `get_pipe_data` still warns and returns `None`, `sync_pipe` still returns `(False, "Failed to post a chunk to ...")` | the message text is `reqwest`'s (`error sending request for url ... client error (Connect)`), not urllib3's |
| W16S-003 | `session` is a `requests.Session` used by every request | `session` creates a `requests.Session` on access (needs `requests`); the connector does not use it. `_token` / `_expires` / `_session` instance attributes are gone: `token` asks the client | D20; nothing in the tree reads them |
| W16S-004 | `get_actions()` returns the `requests.Response` of `GET /actions` | the same shape: the response object, whose `.json()` is the list of action names | kept on purpose (`_actions.py` v4 returned the response); the Rust client also has `get_actions() -> list`, unused by the façade |
| W16S-005 | `sync_pipe(pipe, df)` needs a DataFrame (`df.columns` is read first: a list of documents raises `AttributeError`); `chunksize=None` posts one row per request | also takes documents (a list of dicts), a dictionary of lists, a JSON text, Polars and Arrow frames (`TypeError` for anything else); `chunksize` rules are v4's (`None` is one row per request, `-1` is `system:connectors:sql:chunksize`); keyword arguments are query parameters | the frame goes through the kernel boundary (`input_from_py`); a DataFrame behaves as v4 |
| W16S-006 | `get_pipe_data` returns pandas inferred from JSON | the same values; the connector's own `get_pipe_data` frame has the boundary's default dtypes (`int64` without nulls, nullable `Int64` with nulls, `float64`, `datetime64[us, UTC]`, `object` for text), equal in family and rows to v4's. `Pipe.get_data()` of an `api:` or `valkey:` instance builds the frame the way v4's JSON-built one was before `Pipe.enforce_dtypes` (no dtype hints: `pipe_methods_api::hinted_reads`), so a pipe whose dtypes are inferred from the remote table comes back as `Int64` / `Float64` / `boolean` / `string` as in v4, and declared ones as `int64[pyarrow]` ... as in v4 (`test_get_data_dtypes_match_the_v4_client`: `get_data`, `select_columns`, `params` and chunks, declared and inferred dtypes) | `Pipe.get_data()` enforces the pipe's dtypes either way |
| W16S-007 | `get_user_id` returns an `int`, a `UUID` (if the text is one) or a string | the same; the `UUID` is built by the façade | |
| W16S-008 | `get_token_scopes()` always returns `[]` for an existing token (reads `model.scopes` and then `.scopes` of the list) and raises for a missing one | the same (`// PARITY:` in `meerschaum-connector-api`) | v4 bug kept |
| W16S-009 | `test_connection(**kw)` runs `retry_connect` in a child interpreter (`venv_exec`, `dill`) | the same decisions in-process: the instance must answer `/chaining` (`False` is accepted unless `enforce_chaining`), then `login()` only when `enforce_login`; `max_retries`, `retry_wait`, `warn` honored. Keyword defaults are `retry_connect`'s as `test_connection` passed them (1 try, no wait, no warning, no chaining or login enforcement) | no child interpreter or `dill` (D20) |
| W16S-010 | `get_job_prompt_kwargs` and `get_pipe_index_names` are defined in the API connector's modules but never added to the class | both are class members | `Job.get_prompt_kwargs()` of an `api:` job and the engine's index-name reads call them |
| W16S-011 | `monitor_logs_async` awaits the callbacks in the connector's own `websockets` loop | the Rust follower runs on a worker thread (`run_in_executor`); the callbacks run on the event loop (coroutine functions are awaited there, plain ones are called there), `StopMonitoringLogs` and `KeyboardInterrupt` end the stream quietly, any other exception ends it and is raised to the caller; cancelling the awaiting task closes the websocket within 100 ms; `accept_input` is ignored, as in v4 | no `websockets` (D20). Tests: `test_monitor_logs_*`, `test_a_cancelled_monitor_stops_streaming` |
| W16S-012 | `edit_pipe`, `register_pipe`, `delete_pipe` leave the connector's view of the pipe | they also drop the client's cached copy of the pipe's attributes, existence and columns types (the cache lives in the Rust client, 600 s / 60 s, as the Python `Pipe` cache did); `invalidate_pipe_cache(pipe)` exists on the Rust client for the `Pipe` façade | an edited pipe must not read its stale remote parameters back |
| W16S-013 | `install_plugin` fetches the archive with `wget` and detects a JSON reply with `binaryornot` | the Rust client downloads it (`download_plugin`: a JSON reply, or an error status, is the failure message) and `Plugin.install` installs it | no `binaryornot` |
| W16S-014 | `ValkeyConnector.client` is a `valkey.Valkey` (the package is installed on first use) | a `valkey.Valkey` when the package is importable (never installed by Meerschaum), else a built-in `ValkeyClient` over the Rust client: `get`, `set(ex, px, nx)`, `delete`, `unlink`, `exists`, `incr`, `type`, `keys`, `zadd`, `zrem`, `zcard`, `zrange`, `zrangebyscore`, `sadd`, `srem`, `scard`, `smembers`, `llen`, `rpush`, `lrange`, `hset`, `hgetall`, `flushdb`, `ping`, `execute_command`; strings are `bytes`; any other command raises `NotImplementedError` | the Pipe cache and the session store call `client.keys`, `unlink`, `delete`; the package is not a dependency (D20) |
| W16S-015 | `ValkeyConnector.set(key, value, **kwargs)` forwards to `valkey.Valkey.set` | `ex=` is the Rust client's; other options (`px`, `nx`) go to `client` | |
| W16S-016 | `get_pipe_data` / `read` of a Valkey instance return frames with `object` columns (the documents' values) | typed columns (`datetime64[us, UTC]`, `float64`, `int64`) with the same values and index order; `Pipe.get_data()` enforces dtypes in both | the kernel types its columns |
| W16S-021 | `Pipe.get_data()` of a `valkey:` pipe whose dtypes are inferred returns the datetime column as the Arrow timestamp type the connector stored (`timestamp[us, tz=UTC][pyarrow]`) | `datetime64[us, UTC]`; every other column's dtype (`Int64`, `Float64`, `boolean`, `string`) is v4's (`test_pipe_get_data_dtypes_match_the_v4_connector`) | the boundary always builds datetime columns as `datetime64` (`to_pandas_dtype`) |
| W16S-017 | `sync_pipe(pipe, None)` raises `AttributeError` (`None.__module__`) | `(False, "DataFrame is None. Cannot sync ...")` | no reason to raise |
| W16S-018 | a sync records the new dtypes under `parameters['valkey']['dtypes']` through `pipe.update_parameters(...)`, which also updates the Python pipe | the Rust client persists them; the façade reads them back and patches the Python pipe in memory (`update_parameters(..., persist=False)`) | `get_pipe_columns_types(pipe)` of the same Python object reads them |
| W16S-019 | `_push_hash_docs_to_list`, `_read_docs_from_list` | removed | private, unused |
| W16S-020 | `register_user` sets `user.user_id` | the same (the façade sets it from the Rust client's new ID) | |

Quirks of v4 the oracle runs confirm and the Rust crates keep on purpose (see `// PARITY:` in `meerschaum-connector-valkey`): `end` of `read_docs` is inclusive
(`ZRANGEBYSCORE`) while `read()` and `get_pipe_data()` filter with `query_df` (exclusive); the index entries of cleared rows stay and the unfiltered row
count includes them; `get_sync_time` ignores `params`; the pipes table holds no parameters, so a `tags` filter of `fetch_pipes_keys` matches nothing;
`edit_user` of a user without a `type` fails with the Python client's `Invalid input of type: 'NoneType'` message.

Not covered by an automated test: the `api` flavor on port 8989 (forbidden), TLS (`verify: false`), `register_plugin(make_archive=True)` with a real
`Plugin` archive (a duck-typed plugin with a prebuilt archive is), `install_plugin`.

### Jobs (task S, `jobs_api.rs`): `meerschaum.jobs.Job`, the executors and `utils.daemon` over `meerschaum-jobs`

`Job`, `SystemdExecutor` and the `utils.daemon` functions are façades over `_core.Job`, `_core.Executor`, `_core.Daemon`,
`_core.RotatingFile`, `_core.StdinFile` (`crates/meerschaum-py/src/jobs_api.rs`); the module-level queries are
`_core.jobs_get_jobs`, `jobs_get_filtered_jobs`, `jobs_executor_keys_from_context`, `jobs_daemon_ids`, `jobs_new_daemon_name`.
Every status, PID, signal and layout rule is the Rust type's, so the wave-16 M2 behavior (no `/proc` scan for a job which ended) holds
from Python: `tests/test_jobs.py::test_completed_job_result_logs_and_no_process_scans` asserts `_core.jobs_cmdline_scans()` does not move
over 25 status / PID / result reads of a completed job.

| Behavior | v4.1.4 | v5 | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Program a job runs | the Python process forks and runs `entry(sysargs)` in itself (`Daemon.run`, `daemon.DaemonContext`) | `<mrsm> <sysargs>` under the Rust supervisor (`<mrsm> __mrsm_job_supervisor__ ...`). The binary is `MRSM_JOB_SUPERVISOR` / `MRSM_JOB_PROGRAM` (shell-split) when both are set, else the façade's `meerschaum._internal.binary.find_binary()` (`MRSM_BIN`, the interpreter's scripts directory, `PATH`, a checkout's `target/{release,debug}/mrsm`), else the same lookup in Rust. When nothing is found, every operation which would start a process (`Job.start`, `restart`-driven `check_restart`, `Daemon.run`, `Executor.create_job` / `start_job` for `local` / `systemd`) answers `(False, 'Cannot start jobs: no `mrsm` executable was found. ...')`; reads and stops still work. No Python-hosted supervisor | `current_exe()` inside the extension is the interpreter; a job must be started by the binary (the `meerschaum-cli` wheel installs it next to the interpreter). Test: `test_launcher_error_when_no_binary` | (wave 17: the program is the process's own command, `python -m meerschaum`, `MRSM_BIN` or the standalone binary; no lookup of a `mrsm` next to the interpreter, no `jobs_set_binary`, no "no `mrsm` executable" error)
| `Daemon(target=<any callable>)`, `run_daemon`, `daemon_action`, `daemon_entry`, `Daemon.run()` of a Python function | forks a daemon which runs the callable | removed. `Daemon(target=...)` raises `NotImplementedError` ("... not supported in Meerschaum v5 ...", naming `Job`); only `meerschaum._internal.entry.entry` (a Meerschaum command: `Daemon(target=entry, target_args=[sysargs], daemon_id=...)`, what `Job.daemon` built) and `Daemon(daemon_id=..., sysargs=[...])` are accepted | a forked Python process has no counterpart in a Rust-supervised job (design §4.4: `Daemon` is removed, `Job` stays). Test: `test_daemon_facade_refuses_what_v5_removed` |
| `FileDescriptorInterceptor`, `RotatingFile(redirect_streams=..., write_callback=...)`, `start_log_fd_interception` / `stop_log_fd_interception`, `Daemon.pid_lock`, `log_offset_lock`, `pickle_path`-based `read_pickle` / `write_pickle`, `RotatingFile.is_subfile_too_large` / `increment_subfiles` / `refresh_files` / `get_remaining_subfile_size`, `StdinFile.file_handler` / `read` / `detach` / `newlines` / `errors` / `refresh_seconds`, `SystemdExecutor.get_job_process` (a `psutil.Process`) | exist | removed (`redirect_streams` / `write_callback` raise `NotImplementedError`); the supervisor copies a job's output into its log, and the Rust jobs keep no pickle or lock objects visible to Python. `Daemon.process` is the PID (`int` or `None`), not a `psutil.Process` | the stream interception and `psutil` are the supervisor's / the OS's job now (D20) |
| `Job.__init__(..., _rotating_log=, _stdin_file=)`, `Job.monitor_logs(..., _logs_path=, _log=, _stdin_file=)` | private injection points of the systemd executor's hidden sister job | removed (`_status_hook`, `_result_hook`, `_properties`, `_externally_managed`, `_wait_if_stopped` stay) | the systemd executor is Rust and follows its own log file |
| Where `monitor_logs` callbacks run | on the calling thread (inside `asyncio.run`); `watchfiles` wakes it on a change | the Rust follower polls the log every `system.cli.refresh_seconds` (0.1 s) on a helper thread; callbacks take the GIL there. The calling thread waits with the GIL released, raises `KeyboardInterrupt` for Ctrl-C and watches `stop_event.is_set()` every 50 ms. `monitor_logs_async` runs the same call in the loop's executor; coroutine callbacks are awaited on the loop (sync `monitor_logs` runs them with `asyncio.run`). A callback raising `StopMonitoringLogs` ends monitoring; any other `Exception` is shown with `warn(...)` and monitoring goes on (as v4); a `BaseException` ends it and is re-raised to the caller | no `watchfiles` (D20); the follower is the one the CLI and the API server use. Tests: `test_monitor_logs_*` |
| `get_jobs` / `get_filtered_jobs` of an unreachable or unauthorized executor | `{}` with no message | `{}` after a `warn("Could not get the jobs of executor '<keys>': <cause>")` | wave 15 (M): an empty answer must not be silent; the native CLI fails instead, the Python return shape stays. Test: `test_api_executor_reports_an_unreachable_endpoint` |
| `Job.start()` / `Executor.start_job` on an `api:` job whose endpoint cannot be read | `(False, ...)` only when the POST fails | `(False, <cause>)`: a failed existence read is never taken for "does not exist" | wave 15 (M), now visible from Python |
| `get_executor_keys_from_context()` | caches the computed value, requires `is_installed('meerschaum', allow_outside_venv=False)` | computed per call (cheap), no `is_installed` clause (see wave 15 M) | the binary is always installed |
| `Job.from_pid(pid)` | `psutil`: a daemon's `-c ...daemon_id='x'` command line, or the arguments after `mrsm` / `meerschaum` parsed with `parse_arguments` | the job whose PID is `pid` (any of `get_daemon_ids()`), else the arguments after `mrsm` / `meerschaum` in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` (Linux), taking only `--name` / `--job-name` from them (otherwise a new random name); `ProcessLookupError` (after the same `warn`) when the process does not exist (v4: `psutil.NoSuchProcess`); `EnvironmentError` when the process is not a Meerschaum job | no `psutil`, no argument parser in the façade |
| `Job.result` of a job started in this process | falls back to the in-process `_results` registry when `properties['result']` is empty | the properties only (`// PARITY:` in `job.rs`) | the supervisor of another process writes the result |
| `Job.daemon` of a non-local job | a `Daemon(target=entry, ...)` with the executor's properties | a `Daemon(daemon_id=..., sysargs=..., properties=...)` over the same data (nothing is created on disk) | unchanged semantics, no `entry` import |
| `check_restart_jobs` lock | `InterProcessLock` (`meerschaum.utils.locks`) | `fcntl.flock` on `CHECK_JOBS_LOCK_PATH` | one dependency less |
| Process-wide connectors for `api:<label>` executors | `mrsm.get_connector(keys)` per call | one `CliRemoteResolver` per process and per snapshot of `meerschaum:connectors:api` (login token and metadata cache are shared by every `Job` on that endpoint); a connector defined ad hoc in Python (`mrsm.get_connector('api', 'x', host=...)`) is passed as `api_attributes` and patches its label's section | `jobs_api.rs` builds on `meerschaum-cli`'s resolver; `tests/test_jobs_facade.py::test_api_executor_job_lifecycle` uses an ad hoc connector |
| Cost of `Job(...)` | | ~0.5 ms: the jobs context is cached on a fingerprint of the config sections the jobs read, the root, the launcher inputs and the ad hoc connectors (`ctx_fingerprint`); building it from the live config is ~15 ms | `get_jobs()` of 100 jobs |

Follow-ups (wave 16, P2): `python::core_action` and the worker host's `core_action` operation are deleted (an action without a
native handler now fails with `NotImplementedError: The action ... has no native implementation.`). `meerschaum/compose/**` keeps its
public names (v4.1.4 baseline) as delegations: the subactions (`_compose_up`, ... , `_do_subaction`, `compose`, `complete_compose`) call
`_core.run_action(['compose', ...])`; `utils.config.{read_compose_config, get_env_dict, get_dir_paths, init_env}` and
`utils.plugins.get_installed_plugins` call `_core.compose_{read_config,env_dict,dir_paths,init_env,installed_plugins}`
(`src/compose_api.rs`, over `meerschaum-compose`; paths cross as strings and come back as `pathlib.Path`s). `utils.run_mrsm_command`
always starts the native binary as a child process with the project's environment (`_subprocess` is ignored; the in-process
`replace_config` / `replace_env` isolation of v4 is gone). The helpers that never needed the v4 core (`jobs`, `pipes`, `stack`,
`debug`, `sync`, the config-cache helpers) are unchanged. The Python-body tests of the deleted subaction code (14 in
`tests/test_compose.py`) were removed. `crates/meerschaum-compose/tests/parity.rs` replays the recorded project fixtures (config,
environment, `up`/`down`/`explain` commands) against v4.1.4; it does **not** cover the job-safety cases. Those are ported, one for
one, into `crates/meerschaum-compose/tests/behavior.rs` over the fake jobs/instance/runner harness (`scoped`, `run_with`):
`job_actions_without_names_are_scoped_to_the_project`, `start_jobs_leaves_running_jobs_alone`, `bare_stop_jobs_asks_for_confirmation`,
`job_actions_refuse_other_projects_jobs`, `job_actions_with_no_project_jobs_do_nothing`, `other_actions_pass_through_unscoped`,
`down_deletes_only_exact_owned_job_names`, `down_propagates_delete_failures`,
`down_untags_shared_pipes_and_deletes_only_unshared`, `up_refuses_foreign_collisions_and_propagates_job_start_failures`,
`up_starts_jobs_instead_of_syncing_pipes` (the `--presync --no-jobs` pass included).
The other five deleted tests: `test_compose_init_provisions_plugins_on_first_run` (a fixed regression) is
`behavior.rs::init_provisions_plugins_on_the_first_run` (a runner whose `install plugins` makes the plugin visible; `install
required` and `setup plugins` must follow, in that order); `test_compose_shell_prompt_shows_the_active_project` is
`crates/meerschaum-cli/tests/shell.rs::the_prompt_names_the_active_compose_project` (the native shell names the project of
`MRSM__COMPOSE_CONFIG`, from `project_name` or the compose file's directory, and swaps a pre-4.0 `{executor_keys}` prompt slot).
Three are **obsolete by design** and have no replacement, because what they test no longer exists: 
`test_compose_is_native_and_preserves_arbitrary_action_passthrough` (monkeypatched the Python `_do_subaction`; the passthrough is
`other_actions_pass_through_unscoped`), and `test_compose_restores_host_plugins_after_subaction_error` /
`test_compose_restores_environment_and_plugins_when_host_unload_fails` (the in-process `replace_env` / `replace_config` and
plugin unload/reload isolation of v4; every compose command is a child process with the project's environment, so there is no
host state to restore). `crates/meerschaum-worker/tests/plugins.rs::plugin_actions_match_v4` now records its reference with the released v4
(`MRSM_TEST_PYTHON`, default `.venv-ref`) outside the repository.

## Wave 16 (task L2 follow-up): `GET /pipes/{ck}/{mk}/{lk}/docs`

v4's route is `json.dumps(pipe.get_docs(), default=json_serialize_value)`, and `Pipe.get_docs()` is the instance's own
`get_pipe_docs()`: raw rows with no dtype enforcement. The v5 route read the dtype-enforced frame instead, so every datetime came
out as `2024-01-02T03:04:05+00:00` (a reviewer saw this against SQLite's ` 00:00:00.000000`). Checked against `.venv-ref`
(v4.1.4, temporary root, same rows through each flavor): SQLite `2024-01-02 03:04:05.000000` (the stored text), MySQL / MariaDB
`2024-01-02T03:04:05Z` (naive plus `Z`), PostgreSQL / TimescaleDB `2024-01-02T03:04:05+00:00`, a SQL `NULL` in a float column
`null`.

| ID | Python 4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W16L2-016 | see above | **fixed:** the route calls the instance's `get_pipe_docs` (`routes/pipes.rs::get_pipe_docs`), which `SqlConnector` already answers with the raw rows; every flavor above now matches v4 byte for byte. Instances without their own `get_pipe_docs` (Valkey, custom) keep the trait default, `get_pipe_data()` as documents, as in v4 | tests: `routes.rs::pipe_docs_are_the_instances_raw_documents`, `meerschaum-connector-sql/tests/instance_pipe_data.rs::test_docs_datetime_text_matches_v4` (sqlite, mysql, mariadb, postgresql, postgis, timescaledb) |
| W16L2-017 | a float column's `NULL` is `None`, a stored float `NaN` is `NaN` (invalid JSON) | both are `null`: the frame holds `NaN` for a null float, so the two cannot be told apart (`doc_value`) | the `NaN` token breaks every JSON client; a stored float `NaN` is rare. MSSQL, DuckDB and GeoPackage were not compared against v4 here (`mssql` needs ODBC in the reference interpreter) |

Review follow-ups (wave 16, P2): prompts of the in-process dispatcher read Python's stdin: the extension's `Runtime` host (`PyHost`, `entry_api.rs`) answers `read_line` / `read_line_interruptible` / `read_multiline` / `read_line_prefilled` with `builtins.input(prompt)` and `read_secret` with `getpass.getpass(prompt)`, so a patched `input`, a replaced `sys.stdin` and Jupyter work (v4 used `prompt_toolkit`, which reads the terminal); `EOFError` and `KeyboardInterrupt` end the prompt like the end of input; processes (`run`, `output`) and timed input stay the binary's. `_internal/binary.py` passes `MRSM_PYTHON` to the child through `env=` (this process's environment is not modified) and warns (`RuntimeWarning`) about an unusable `MRSM_BIN`. `_internal/_removed.py` gives a removed module a spec whose loader raises, so `importlib.util.find_spec()` probes do not raise and importing does. `compose_api.rs` and `Runtime::system` skip environment variables that are not valid UTF-8 (`std::env::vars()` panics on them). `import meerschaum.api` outside the API host installs nothing, checks no package versions, prints no tracebacks (a missing uvicorn config file is the normal case, and is read once) and raises one `ImportError` naming the `api` extra when FastAPI, fastapi-login or python-jose is missing.

Re-review follow-ups (wave 16, P2): **stop is per call.** `native::jobs::with_call_stop` gives the thread that runs a call its own stop flag, which `stop_requested_flag()` returns there (the binary, which sets none, still has the process-wide flag its signal handler sets); `run_interruptible` no longer resets anything process-wide. A loop abandoned by a second Ctrl-C keeps its flag set and ends at its next checkpoint, a later `entry()` cannot revive it, and concurrent calls from several threads stop independently. A call made on a non-main thread cannot be interrupted (Python delivers signals to the main thread); a stopped call asks no further prompts. A prompt already blocked in `input()` when its call is abandoned cannot be cancelled: the line typed next is consumed by it (documented limit). **No worker while a worker imports a plugin.** The guard is held by the code that imports plugins, not by the entry points: the worker program (`host.py`) marks its interpreter (`_core.set_in_worker()`, `meerschaum.plugins._IN_WORKER`), `meerschaum.plugins.plugin_import_guard()` (around `import_plugins`, the import in `load_plugins` and `from_plugin_import`) counts the imports in progress (`_core.plugin_import_begin/end`: a counter, because imports nest and overlap), and `Worker::spawn` refuses to start a worker while both hold (`spawn_refused()`). One place therefore covers `mrsm.entry()`, `Pipe.sync()`, `verify()` and `Pipeline`: a plugin whose import calls `mrsm.entry()` or syncs a `plugin:` pipe no longer starts workers without end (v4 imported plugins in-process, so it could not recurse; the refused sync simply fails). `_core.entry` prints `Plugins are skipped for this call: it was made while a plugin was being imported in a worker ...` and runs natively without plugins. Nothing is refused outside a worker's own import: a plugin action may run other plugins' actions and `sync pipes -c plugin:<src>`, and the main process starts workers freely (its in-process imports start at most one generation: that worker's own imports are guarded). The refusal is process-wide while a worker imports a plugin, so a sync another thread of that worker starts during the import is refused too; imports happen once per module. The detection by `__spec__._initializing` is gone (the remaining uses of it in `plugins._get_loaded_module` and `utils.packages` are v4's, and only skip half-imported modules). Also: every `std::env::vars()` reachable from the extension (`Pipe.verify()`'s runtime, the systemd unit text, `jobs_backend`, the compose worker environment, `get_service_file_text`) skips variables that are not valid UTF-8 instead of panicking. Tests `test_a_plugin_that_calls_entry_when_imported_does_not_start_workers_without_end` (peak descendant processes <= 8, prompt return), `test_a_plugin_that_syncs_a_plugin_pipe_when_imported_keeps_the_process_count_bounded` (peak 3 processes; a `/proc` watchdog kills everything carrying the test's marker if more than 30 appear), `test_an_undecodable_environment_variable_does_not_panic_verify_or_the_systemd_text`, `test_a_plugin_action_can_run_another_plugin_action_and_sync_a_plugin_source` (results equal the ones recorded from v4.1.4; `MRSM_TEST_V4_LIVE=1` re-records them with `.venv-ref`, which takes minutes because v4 builds its venv), `test_after_a_second_ctrl_c_a_new_entry_does_not_revive_the_old_loop`, `test_concurrent_entries_have_their_own_stop`.
| W16Q-004 | any user registers or edits a token with any scopes (`api/routes/_tokens.py`, `register tokens`, `edit tokens`, the console), `*` included; omitted scopes default to the built-in list | **a token carries at most its owner's scopes**, rejected (never trimmed): `POST /tokens/register` and `POST /tokens/{id}/edit` answer `403 Cannot grant scopes you do not hold: '<scope>', ...` (`auth::grantable_scopes`, `disallowed_scopes`, `scope_cap_error`; `routes/tokens.rs`), `register tokens` / `edit tokens` fail with `Cannot grant scopes that user '<owner>' does not hold: ...` for non-`api` instances (an `api` instance enforces it server-side) (`native/tokens.rs::grant_cap`), and the console register form starts from the signed-in user's scopes with the switch on only when they may grant everything (`pages.rs::tokens`). An administrator or a holder of `*` may grant anything (`*` and custom scopes included); no scopes asked for means the owner's own (the built-in list for an administrator or `*` holder, as v4); an edit keeps the scopes the token already holds (renaming never fails); `--no-auth` has no owner and no cap. The Python `Token.register()` / instance methods talk to the instance directly and are not capped | user-requested hardening. Tests: `routes.rs::a_token_carries_at_most_its_owners_scopes`, `residual_actions_a.rs::a_token_carries_at_most_its_owners_scopes`, `console.rs::the_token_form_starts_from_the_owners_scopes_and_refuses_more` |
| W16Q-005 | no warning when open self-registration (`api:permissions:registration:users`, default true) and terminals for non-admins (`api:permissions:actions:non_admin`, default true) make the API a shell for anyone who can connect | behavior kept (v4 parity, W13-E); `start api` prints one warning through the formatter when both are on, the console and terminal are served (no `--no-dash` / `--no-webterm` / `--no-auth`), neither `--secure` nor `--private` is given and the bind address is not loopback (`ServerConfig::open_terminal_warning`, `cli/src/server.rs`); the migration guide, API deployment page and web console page carry a danger admonition | user decision (2026-10-17). Tests: `config.rs::open_registration_with_non_admin_terminals_warns_off_loopback`, `server_modes.rs::start_api_warns_once_when_anyone_who_can_connect_could_get_a_shell` |
| W16Q-006 | `POST /tokens/{id}/edit`, `POST /tokens/{id}/invalidate` and `DELETE /tokens/{id}` never check the token's owner (v4.1.4 `api/routes/_tokens.py`): any logged-in user relabels or re-scopes another user's token (an administrator's `*` token included), deletes it, or re-validates one an administrator invalidated; an edit without `scopes` sets the built-in list; an edit without `is_valid` makes the token valid; `edit` stamps the caller as `token.user` (a no-op: `default_edit_token` never writes `user_id`) | owner-or-administrator on all three (`routes/tokens.rs::owned_token`): `403 Cannot edit|invalidate|delete another user's token.`, `404` for a missing token, an ownerless token is an administrator's, no check under `--no-auth`; only an administrator may set `is_valid` from false to true (`403 Only an administrator may make an invalidated token valid again.`); an edit that leaves out `is_valid` or `scopes` (or sends no scopes) keeps the token's own; the caller stamp is dropped. The CLI token actions (`edit tokens`) and the Python instance methods act on the instance directly with the operator's own access and stay trusted (documented in `native/tokens.rs`); an `api:` instance enforces the rule server-side | review (security; also v4.1.4 behavior): the owner check existed only on `GET /tokens/{id}`. Test: `routes.rs::tokens_can_only_be_changed_by_their_owner_or_an_administrator` (another user, administrator, owner, re-validation, missing token) |
| W16Q-007 | no warning for `--no-auth` on a non-loopback bind | one startup warning through the formatter: `ServerConfig::open_no_auth_warning` (`start api`, not `start mcp`); the open-terminal warning (W16Q-005) stays quiet under `--no-auth` because there is no terminal. Neither warning sees a reverse proxy (a loopback bind behind one exposes the same routes silently; the docs say so) | review. Tests: `config.rs::no_auth_off_loopback_warns`, `server_modes.rs::start_api_warns_once_for_no_auth_off_loopback` |


Wave 16b (P2): **A refused import-time sync is a failed sync.** While a worker imports a plugin no worker is started (see above), so a `Pipe.sync()` in a plugin's module body that needs one (a `plugin:` source) used to raise `RuntimeError` out of `_core.sync_pipe`, and the plugin failed to import. `sync_pipe` now returns the failed result (`(False, 'failed to start the Python worker: not starting a Python worker while a plugin is being imported ...')`) when `Worker::spawn` refused, as it does for any sync that cannot run, so the import completes and the plugin's `fetch` and actions stay available (a worker that serves a plugin *action* routes the sync through the host's callback instead, which succeeds). Other bindings that start a worker (`pipe_get_data` ...) still raise `RuntimeError` for the refusal. Test: the `w16syncimp` plugin of `test_a_plugin_that_syncs_a_plugin_pipe_when_imported_keeps_the_process_count_bounded` logs the tuple of its refused sync and then serves an action and a `fetch`. **Non-UTF-8 environment.** `meerschaum_core::process_env::vars()` (`std::env::vars_os()` without the variables that are not valid UTF-8; `std::env::vars()` panics on them) is the one reader of the whole environment: `ConfigOptions::from_process` (the `mrsm` binary exited 101 on `W16BAD=$'\xff\xfe' mrsm show version`), the compose process environment, the webterm tmux environment file, `meerschaum_cli::dispatch::process_vars()` and the `meerschaum-py` build script use it. Test: `crates/meerschaum-cli/tests/non_utf8_env.rs`.

## Wave 17 (task D): DuckDB loaded at run time

The `duckdb` crate (bundled C++ build) is gone. `meerschaum-connector-sql` binds DuckDB's own shared library (`libduckdb`,
v1.4 or newer; the installer fetches v1.5.6) through its C API with `libloading`
(`driver/duckdb/ffi.rs`, `decode.rs`; lookup and download in `duckdb_lib/`). Results are read from data chunks instead of the
crate's `ValueRef` rows; the cell types, column kinds and type names are the old ones except as listed.

| ID | Bundled DuckDB 1.4 (before) | Run-time `libduckdb` | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17D-001 | an aware timestamp parameter (`SqlParam::TimestampTz`) was bound as a naive `TIMESTAMP` of its UTC microseconds, which DuckDB reads in the session `TimeZone` when it meets a `TIMESTAMPTZ` | bound with `duckdb_create_timestamp_tz`: the instant is kept whatever the session zone is | with ICU the naive value was shifted by the zone offset; the bundled build had no ICU, so every session was UTC and the bug could not show. Tests: `tests/duckdb_library.rs::aware_timestamps_keep_their_instant_in_a_non_utc_session`, `driver::duckdb::tests::binds_dates_and_aware_timestamps_natively` |
| W17D-002 | no ICU extension: sessions ran in UTC, `AT TIME ZONE` and named zones failed | the official library bundles ICU; every connection runs `SET TimeZone = 'UTC'` so a naive timestamp still means UTC on any host (without this the instance sync tests shifted by the host offset) | keeps the old, host-independent behavior; `current_setting('TimeZone')` is `UTC`. Tests: `sessions_start_in_utc`, `icu_is_available` |
| W17D-003 | a list, struct, map, union, array, enum, interval, bit string or `TIME WITH TIME ZONE` value read as Rust `Debug` text of the crate's `Value` (e.g. `List([Int(1), Int(2)])`) | DuckDB's own text, identical to `CAST(x AS VARCHAR)` row by row (strings quoted inside lists, structs and maps only when DuckDB quotes them; tested against the cast): (`[1, 2]`, `{'a': 1}`, `1 year 2 months 3 days 04:00:00`, `1010`, `10:11:12+02`); the column is `VARCHAR`-kind with the type name `LIST`, `STRUCT`, `ENUM`, ... | the old text was an accident of `Debug`; DuckDB's casts to `VARCHAR` are what users see in its shell. Test: `nested_and_special_types_read_as_duckdb_text` |
| W17D-004 | a `BIGNUM` / `VARINT` column read as `Debug` text of an unsupported value | its decimal text (`10`, `-300`, 31 digits exact) in a `Utf8` column named `BIGNUM` | exact value. Tests: `driver::duckdb::tests::decodes_bignum`, `nested_and_special_types_read_as_duckdb_text` |
| W17D-005 | `HUGEINT` read as a `DECIMAL(38,0)` column, which cannot hold its 39-digit extremes: the largest `HUGEINT` failed the whole query (`cannot convert Text to decimal`) | `HUGEINT` and `UHUGEINT` are exact numeric text (`Utf8` tagged `meerschaum:dtype=numeric`, type names `HUGEINT` / `UHUGEINT`); a decimal wider than `rust_decimal`'s 96-bit mantissa still reads as exact text | exactness and no failure on valid data. Tests: `wide_integers_uuids_decimals_and_times_decode_on_every_row`, `scalar_types_decode` |
| W17D-008 | `infinity` / `-infinity` timestamps and dates failed the whole query (`timestamp out of range`) | read as 9999-12-31 23:59:59.999999 / 0001-01-01 00:00:00 (dates 9999-12-31 / 0001-01-01), with one warning per query. **The bounds are stored, not the infinity:** a pipe synced onward from DuckDB to another database (or read into pandas) carries `9999-12-31` / `0001-01-01` (`datetime.max` / `datetime.min`), and the target never sees `infinity` | what the Python `duckdb` package (v4's driver) returns: `datetime.max` / `datetime.min`. Test: `infinite_timestamps_and_dates_read_as_the_python_limits` |
| W17D-009 | n/a | a relative `MRSM_DUCKDB_LIB` is made absolute before it is checked and loaded; a failed implicit download is remembered for the life of the process (no 30 s connection timeout per connection offline); on macOS and Windows a library is only loaded from an absolute system directory (Homebrew / `/usr/local/lib`, the absolute `PATH` entries) with the restricted DLL search flags, never by bare name from the working directory; `show drivers` reports a system-path library by path without loading it; the installer trusts the OS root store as well as the bundled roots (TLS-intercepting proxies) | hardening from review. Tests: `duckdb_lib::tests::*`, `client_libs::tests::*`, `show_drivers_does_not_load_a_system_library` |
| W17D-006 | DuckDB was always available in `mrsm` and the extension (compiled in) | found through `MRSM_DUCKDB_LIB`, `<root>/drivers/duckdb/<version>/`, the system loader path, else downloaded once (SHA-256 pinned in `duckdb_lib/install.rs`); `MRSM_NO_AUTO_INSTALL` makes a missing library an error naming `mrsm install driver duckdb` and `MRSM_DUCKDB_LIB`. New actions: `install driver duckdb [--force]`, `uninstall driver duckdb`, `show drivers` | v4 installed packages on first use; the base install stays small (the compiled-in C++ added ~35 MB to the binary and the extension and minutes to every cold build) |

## Wave 17 (task W): one wheel, `_native.main`

D8 is revised: PyPI has one wheel, `meerschaum`; there is no `meerschaum-cli` wheel. `_native.main(argv)`
(`main_api.rs`) runs the same program as the standalone `mrsm` (`meerschaum_cli::run_main`); the `mrsm` and
`meerschaum` console scripts and `python -m meerschaum` call it (`meerschaum/__main__.py`,
`_internal/binary.py`). `cargo install meerschaum` builds the standalone binary from the front crate's `cli`
feature.

| ID | Before (`meerschaum-cli` binary wheel) | One wheel | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17W-001 | `python -m meerschaum` / the console script `execve`d the `mrsm` binary found through `MRSM_BIN`, the scripts directory, `PATH` or `target/` | the CLI runs inside the interpreter (`_native.main`): real file descriptors, the GIL released for the whole run, SIGINT reset to its default action for the run and Python's handler restored if `main` returns to a caller, `MRSM_PYTHON` defaulted to `sys.executable` for the run. `MRSM_BIN` (an executable program, not a `#!` script) still `execve`s that binary | one copy of the core |
| W17W-002 | jobs, compose steps, pipeline children, `systemd` units and the web terminal started `current_exe()` | every one asks `meerschaum_core::self_exe::self_command()`: `[current_exe]` in the standalone binary, `[sys.executable, "-m", "meerschaum"]` in the extension (or `[MRSM_BIN]`). The `systemd` unit's `ExecStart=` is v4's `<python> -m meerschaum <sysargs>` and pins `MRSM_PYTHON` to that interpreter; both shapes of the line are read back | jobs run from either kind of install and stay controllable from the other (job layout unchanged; process markers are arguments, not executable names) |
| W17W-003 | `Job.start()` without a binary answered `(False, 'Cannot start jobs: no `mrsm` executable was found...')` | cannot happen: the interpreter is always there. `jobs_set_binary` and `Ctx.launch_error` are gone; `_core.jobs_launcher()` returns `(supervisor, program)` | |
| W17W-004 | `import meerschaum` imported the whole façade (about 0.12 s) | still EAGER by default (a lazy package deadlocked threads which import `meerschaum.*` while others first read `mrsm.Pipe`: `_DeadlockError` on `_ModuleLock`). Lazy (PEP 562; `_internal/_facade.py` holds the imports) only in the CLI process: `sys._meerschaum_cli` (set by `_meerschaum_mrsm`, the console scripts' top-level module) or `python -m meerschaum` (`sys.orig_argv`). There `from meerschaum import _core` and other submodules never load it; `dir()`, `__all__` and `from meerschaum import *` do. Both modes import every module under `meerschaum` first exactly as before (`tests/test_lazy_package.py`, incl. a threaded regression) | `mrsm` starts without paying for `Pipe` |
| W17W-006 | `main` returned with the CLI's SIGTERM handler installed and SIGINT's replaced | `_native.main` saves and restores the caller's OS-level SIGTERM action (`SIG_DFL` included) and Python's SIGINT handler, keeps the CLI's own handlers for the next run, and refuses a non-main thread (`RuntimeError`); an empty `sys.executable` is an error (`self_command`) | the caller keeps working after `Shell.cmdloop()`; the console script maps an exit status outside 0-255 to 1 |
| W17W-005 | the extension's `tracing` sink always forwarded to `meerschaum.utils.warnings.warn()` | in `_native.main` the sink is off (or a stderr `fmt` subscriber when `RUST_LOG` is set), exactly like the binary, and the API client's warning sink is the CLI's queue; both are put back when `main` returns | the CLI prints its own diagnostics |
| W17D-007 | the in-place sync (`sync_pipe_inplace`) of a DuckDB pipe whose source adds a column failed with `Referenced column "a" not found` (the three `instance_inplace` DuckDB tests were skipped as a "known gap") | fixed: like SQLite, the staged tables are committed before the `ALTER TABLE ... ADD` queries and a new session resumes afterwards (`instance/inplace.rs`) | DuckDB transactions are snapshots: the open session began before another connection added the column, so it never saw it (PostgreSQL and MySQL read committed data). The skips are gone: `test_sync_inplace`, `test_sync_inplace_upsert` and `test_sync_inplace_no_datetime` run on DuckDB |
| W17W-007 | a re-launched `python -m meerschaum` (jobs, compose, pipeline steps, `systemd` units, the web terminal) and the worker / API-host interpreters ran with the working directory first on `sys.path`; v4's `mrsm` console script never had it there | every spawn passes `-P` (`<python> -P -m meerschaum`, `-P -c <host.py>`, `-P -m meerschaum.api.host`; unit line `ExecStart=<python> -P -m meerschaum <sysargs>`) | a `meerschaum/` or `meerschaum.py` in the user's directory would replace the package (verified). Plugins import through the plugins directory, never the working one. Tests: `tests/test_self_command.py`, `meerschaum-worker/tests/shadow_cwd.rs` |
| W17W-008 | `main()` of a script which had imported `meerschaum` eagerly ended in `sys.exit` (v4) | still `sys.exit` there; `os._exit` only in the lazy CLI process (`mrsm`, `python -m meerschaum`) | `atexit` handlers and buffered files of the calling script must run. Test: `tests/test_self_command.py` |
| W17W-009 | `MRSM_BIN` naming a launcher for the console script (pip's `mrsm.exe`) made `mrsm` and every job start themselves forever | the Python side sets `MRSM_BIN_EXEC=<MRSM_BIN>` when it hands over, and a process that sees the guard equal to `MRSM_BIN` ignores it. Edge: a real standalone binary started this way inherits the guard, so a Python plugin in its worker that starts a job uses `<python> -P -m meerschaum`, not the binary | no way to tell a launcher from a binary by its file; `sys.executable` which is not a Python (mod_wsgi, uWSGI) falls back to `sys._base_executable` or is an error naming `MRSM_BIN` |
| W17T-001 | token `scopes` read through `Pipe.get_value()` / `get_doc()` on an instance that returns raw documents (SQLite stores the `json` column as text) | v4.1.4: `get_token_scopes()` returned the text `'["pipes:read"]'`, `get_token_model()` raised `scopes: Input should be a valid list`, the login route did substring checks on the string. v5 (Python façade `connectors/instance/_tokens.py`, Rust `InstanceConnector::get_token_scopes`) | FIX (intentional v4 change): scopes are always a list of strings (`_scopes_list()`, a JSON text is parsed); `Pipe.get_doc()` / `get_value()` stay raw as in v4 | `tests/test_tokens.py`, `instance_users.rs::test_tokens_roundtrip` |
| W17T-002 | `InstanceConnector.get_token()` and `get_token_model(Token)` | v4.1.4: `get_token()` built `Token(**dict(model))` without `instance=`, so the token (and `token.get_scopes(refresh=True)` for a `mrsm-key:` request) resolved on the DEFAULT instance, not the connector it was read from; `get_token_model(token)` read the class attribute `Token.id`. v5 passes `instance=self` and uses `token_id.id` | FIX (intentional v4 change). In the API server this is the code path (`api/_oauth2.py::get_granted_scopes` -> `Token.get_scopes(refresh=True)`) by which v4.1.4 answers 403 to a `mrsm-key:` token on an instance other than the default one (scopes read as `[]`); v5 resolves the token on the server's instance (HTTP 200 for a `pipes:read` token, 403 for `pipes:write`) | `tests/test_tokens.py::test_api_key_of_a_regular_users_token_reads_pipes_on_the_servers_instance` |
| W17T-003 | the CLI progress spinner when SIGINT / SIGTERM ends the process while it is drawn | v4's progress is a rich/spinner thread; v5 (16c) left the last frame on the terminal | FIX: a guard writes `\r\x1b[2K` to fd 2 and re-raises the signal with its default action (exit status unchanged), only while a line is drawn and only when the signal still has its default action; the stop handler's second-signal exit clears it too | `crates/meerschaum-cli/tests/progress_interrupt.rs` |
| W17E-001 | `MRSM_SQL_<LABEL>=<uri>` on a label whose default config is another flavor (`main` = `timescaledb-ha` on `localhost:5432`, `local` = `sqlite`), or any `uri` attribute next to `host` / `port` / `username` / `password` / `database` | v4.1.4 reads the env URI into the label's config (`uri` merged over the default attributes, so `get_connector('sql:main')` reports `flavor='timescaledb-ha'`, `host='localhost'`, `port=5432`, `database='meerschaum'`) but builds its ENGINE from the `uri` alone (`_create_engine.py:88-115`), so it always connects to the URI's database (for a scheme of another family it then generates SQL for the label's flavor and fails on use). v5 (before) chose the Rust driver by the `flavor` attribute and took every connection parameter the URI omits from the attributes: `MRSM_SQL_MAIN=sqlite:////x.db` made the PostgreSQL driver dial `localhost:5432` (the default `sql:main` host) with the SQLite path as the database name, in the Python façade (`_core.SqlConnector`) and in the Rust CLI/server (`-i sql:main`) alike; a server URI without port or password inherited the default's `5432` / `mrsm` | FIX (intentional v4 change, safety + parity): with a `uri`, `ConnectionSpec::from_attributes` takes `host` / `port` / `username` / `password` / `database` from the URI alone (`None` when it omits one, then the flavor default port), like v4's engine. The flavor: a URI of the SAME driver family keeps the configured flavor (`// PARITY:` `timescaledb-ha` for a `postgresql://` URI on `main`, `mariadb` for `mysql://`, `geopackage` for `sqlite:`; as v4 reports it); a URI of ANOTHER family takes the URI's flavor (v4 keeps the label's flavor and breaks), in Rust and in the façade's `SQLConnector.__init__` (`_flavor_family`), so `read()` / `exec()` and the Rust methods use one dialect. `show connectors` keeps printing v4's attributes (default config + `uri`) | `crates/meerschaum-connector-sql/tests/w17b_uri_wins.rs`, `tests/test_w17b_env_uri.py` |
| W17H-001 | `meerschaum.api.manager` / `Depends(manager)` and the scope decoder in `meerschaum.api._oauth2` (`fastapi_login.LoginManager` + `python-jose` in v4, `meerschaum/api/_login.py` + PyJWT in v5) | v4.1.4: `manager` was `fastapi_login.LoginManager`, which itself decodes with PyJWT `jwt.decode(token, secret, algorithms=['HS256'])` (defaults: no leeway, `exp`/`nbf`/`iat`/`aud`/`sub` verified); `get_granted_scopes()` and the host's internal-token check used `jose.jwt.decode(token, SECRET, algorithms=['HS256'])`. v5 `LoginManager` is a port of fastapi-login's header/cookie path (same constructor, `user_loader()`, `optional`, `create_access_token()`, `attach_middleware()`, same `InvalidCredentialsException` 401 `Invalid credentials` / `InsufficientScopeException` 400, same `sub` -> user-loader flow) and `_oauth2.decode_jwt()` (PyJWT, `algorithms=['HS256']`, **no leeway, default claim verification**, the choice that matches fastapi-login exactly) serves the scope decoder and `X-Mrsm-Host-Token` check. Probed against `.venv-ref` (python-jose 3.5.0, PyJWT 2.13) and the CI venv (PyJWT 2.15.1) with tokens signed by the shared secret: valid, expired by 10 s and 60 s, `nbf` +10 s, `iat` +10 s, integer `sub`, missing `sub`, missing `exp`, `aud`, HS384/HS512, garbage, tampered signature. | MANAGER PATH (`Depends(manager)`): identical to v4 (same library, same defaults; leeway 0; non-string `sub` and future `nbf`/`iat` rejected as 401 `Invalid credentials`; missing `sub`/`exp` accepted then `sub is None` -> 401). Tests: `tests/api_host/test_login_manager.py`. SCOPE / INTERNAL-TOKEN PATH (`get_granted_scopes`, `decode_internal_token`): same accept/reject result for every probed token except one: a token whose `iat` is in the future is now rejected (PyJWT `ImmatureSignatureError`; jose accepted it, the Rust server also only type-checks `iat`); tokens minted by the Rust server carry `sub`/`scopes`/`exp`, so none is affected. HTTP behavior is unchanged (401 `Invalid access token.` / 403 on the internal routes). Observable by a plugin: only (a) the exception classes of the private names, since `_oauth2.jose_jwt`, `jose_exceptions` and `fastapi_login` are gone (use `_oauth2.decode_jwt()` / `jwt.PyJWTError`; the jose errors were `JWTError`/`JWTClaimsError`/`ExpiredSignatureError`) and (b) the future-`iat` case above. Dropped `meerschaum.api` re-exports (`aiofiles`, `multipart`, `packaging_version`, `importlib_metadata`, `typing_extensions`, `uvicorn_workers`) were unused by the host; the migration guide's "API plugins" section lists all six. | Implemented; `tests/api_host` (28) pass in the CI venv. `api` extra gained `websockets` (uvicorn serves plugin WebSocket routes only with it; `tests/api_host/test_fresh_venv.py` checks a 101 upgrade through the Rust proxy) |

### Wave 17b (task O): Oracle

| ID | v4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17O-001 | `get_pipe_columns_types` on Oracle reflects the table through SQLAlchemy (`str(column.type)`: `INTEGER` for `NUMBER` with no precision and scale 0, a bare `NUMBER` for every other `NUMBER`, `DOUBLE PRECISION` / `REAL` / `FLOAT`, `NVARCHAR(n)`, `VARCHAR(n)`, `CHAR(n)`, `RAW`, `TIMESTAMP` for every timestamp type) | the same mapping (`instance/reflect.rs::oracle_reflected_type`, `OracleDialect.get_multi_columns`), read from `all_tab_cols` for the current schema and the denormalized table name; before, v5 returned the `all_tab_columns` strings (`NUMBER`, `TIMESTAMP(6)`), so an `INT` column was never widened for a float (`2.1` stored as `2`) and the pandas dtype hints differed | parity fix (bug in the port, not a deviation). Tests: `instance_dtypes.rs`, `instance_sync.rs::test_sync_null_indices` on Oracle, `reflect::tests::oracle_types` |
| W17O-002 | no internal (`mrsm`) pipes | internal string keys on Oracle are `NVARCHAR2(255)` (like `VARCHAR(255)` / `NVARCHAR(255)` on MySQL, MariaDB and MSSQL) and a longer key is refused with a message; the unique index over two `NVARCHAR2(2000)` columns of an engine state pipe was 8000 bytes, over Oracle's key limit (about 6398 bytes at an 8 KB block): `ORA-01450` | v5-only feature; the bound is the one the other bounded flavors use. Tests: `w5sql.rs`, `w6b_templates.rs::internal_key_length_bound` (Oracle now expects the refusal) |
| W17O-003 | `sql_item_name` quotes ten Oracle words (`float`, `varchar`, `nvarchar`, `clob`, `boolean`, `integer`, `table`, `row`, `date`, `time`); a column named `uid`, `user`, `level`, `comment`, `size`, `start`, ... is emitted bare and `CREATE TABLE` fails with `ORA-00904` (checked on the Oracle test container with v4.1.4: `uid CHAR(36)`) | intentional fix: Oracle's true reserved words are quoted too (`ident.rs::ORACLE_RESERVED_EXTRA`: the documented "Oracle SQL Reserved Words" table, verified on Oracle XE 21c against every keyword of `V$RESERVED_WORDS`, 103 more words) | cannot change a name v4 created, because v4 could not create it. The v4.1.4 golden fixtures are unchanged; `golden.rs` accepts exactly the quotes of these words on the Oracle flavor (`without_intentional_oracle_quoting`) and every other difference still fails. Test: `w9u_pg_params.rs::internal_upsert_binds_typed_cells` (a `uid` column) |
| W17O-004 | MSSQL pipe-keys tag filter: SQLAlchemy renders `cast(parameters, String)` as `CAST(parameters AS VARCHAR(max))` | the same; before, `CAST(parameters AS VARCHAR)` is `VARCHAR(30)` on MSSQL, so a tag after the first 30 characters of the parameters never matched (`mrsm show pipes -t <tag>`, `get_pipes(tags=...)`, compose projects returned nothing) | parity fix. Tests: `instance_pipes_keys.rs::test_fetch_pipes_keys_tags_after_long_parameters` (every flavor), `tests/test_get_pipes_tags.py` |

Continuing..." and syncs on the primary key (checked on the Oracle test container with v4.1.4) | the redundant pair is not emitted when Oracle's existing primary key is the whole unique key (`queries/index.rs`, `primary_key_is_the_unique_key`) | v5's internal (`mrsm`) pipes treat an index failure as fatal, and they are exactly this shape. ## Wave 17b (task D2): pyo3 0.30, arrow 60, polars 0.55, no pyo3-arrow / pyo3-polars

pyo3 0.25 -> 0.30.1, arrow 56 -> 60.0.0, polars 0.51 -> 0.55.2 (`polars-arrow` 0.55 for
`polars_roundtrip`), num-bigint 0.4 -> 0.5 (core and this crate). `pyo3-arrow` and `pyo3-polars` are
gone: `ArrowStream` exports through `ffi_export.rs` (arrow-rs FFI + `PyCapsule`, same `requested_schema`
rule as pyo3-arrow), `polars_roundtrip` reads and writes the Arrow C stream with Polars' own FFI
(`polars_bridge.rs`), and the crate feature `polars`, `--no-polars` and `_core.HAS_POLARS` are removed.
Plugins built against the arrow-56 `meerschaum-plugin` still load and exchange data (the C ABI is the
standard Arrow C stream; checked with an old `native_demo` build against the new host).

| ID | Before | Now | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17D2-001 | `numeric[0,0]`, a precision above 38 or a scale above the precision (`numeric[5,10]`) on the native enforce path built a Polars `Decimal(..)` that Polars 0.51 accepted (an all-null `numeric[5,10]` became an invalid `Decimal(5, 10)` column) | Polars 0.55 decimals require 1 <= precision <= 38 and scale <= precision, so `native_target` refuses those dtypes and the column takes the unbounded-numeric path: pandas keeps the values (`[Decimal('1.5'), Decimal('0')]`, `object`; all-null becomes `Decimal('NaN')`, as in v4), `as_polars=True` types them from the values (`Decimal(2, 1)`, all-null `Null`) | what v4.1.4 does: its `polars.Decimal(p, s)` cast raises ("precision must be between 1 and 38", "scale must be less than or equal to precision") and `_enforce_dtypes_with_polars` returns `None` (`meerschaum/utils/dataframe.py`). Tests: kernel `numeric_precision_outside_polars_range_has_no_native_target`, `tests/utils/test_dataframe.py::test_enforce_numeric_outside_polars_decimals_is_unbounded` (values recorded from 4.1.4) |
| W17D2-002 | `polars_roundtrip` (pyo3-polars 0.24) needed pyarrow at call time and returned an `Enum` column as `Categorical` (strict xfail) | no pyarrow; every Polars type round-trips, `Enum` included | Polars' own FFI keeps the field metadata. Tests: `test_core.py::test_polars_roundtrip[*]`, `..._empty_frame_keeps_schema`, `..._rejects_objects_without_a_stream` |
| W17D2-003 | `build_info()['features']` listed `polars` in the shipped build; `_core.HAS_POLARS` existed | neither (internal symbols) | the feature is gone. `python_abi` is read from pyo3's target ABI (`abi3-py311`, `abi3t-py3XX`, `cp3XX[t]`); reading the deprecated fields under pyo3 0.30 reported the interpreter's version (`abi3-py314`) |
| W17D2-004 | pyo3 0.25 argument and type errors (`'int' object cannot be converted to 'PyString'`) | pyo3 0.30 wording (`... is not an instance of 'str'`; argument-extraction context is added as a note) | upstream change (pyo3 0.27 `cast`, 0.29 `add_note`); no test or façade matches the text |
| W17D2-005 | an object column of `Decimal`s below 1 became a Polars decimal with one integer digit too many (`0.000012345` -> `Decimal(10, 9)`, `0.5` -> `Decimal(2, 1)`): `as_polars` results and the Arrow-backed pandas dtypes built from them | Polars' own inference, as v4 gets it from `pl.from_pandas`: the most integer digits (none below 1) + the largest scale, never below precision 1 (`Decimal(9, 9)`, `Decimal(1, 1)`; zeros `Decimal(1, 0)`) | parity fix found by W17D2-001's test (values recorded from 4.1.4 with polars 1.44.1). Tests: `crates/meerschaum-kernel/tests/decimal_inference.rs`, `tests/utils/test_dataframe.py::test_enforce_numeric_outside_polars_decimals_is_unbounded` |

| W17O-007a | PostgreSQL: `sql <label> "CREATE VIEW ... AS SELECT ..."` also goes through `read()`. v4 runs `read()` inside `with self.engine.begin() as transaction` (`connectors/sql/_sql.py:322-329`; only `SKIP_READ_TRANSACTION_FLAVORS = ['mssql']` skips it), `pd.read_sql_query` raises SQLAlchemy's `ResourceClosedError` after the statement ran, and the `with` block rolls back, so on PostgreSQL (transactional DDL) the view is NOT kept: a silent v4 bug (read from the v4.1.4 code; not run, `.venv-ref` has no PostgreSQL driver). MySQL / MariaDB / Oracle / SQLite commit DDL implicitly and keep it | v5 creates the view on every flavor, keeps it, and prints `None` after the same message (intentional: a `CREATE VIEW` the user asked for should not vanish) | maintainer decision (2026-10-18).
