| W17O-009 | an unconstrained Oracle `NUMBER` (precision 0: `COUNT(*)`, `1 + 1`, `3 / 2`, a column declared as plain `NUMBER`) reads through python-oracledb as an `int` when the value is integral and a `float` otherwise, per value (`mrsm sql <oracle> "SELECT COUNT(*) ..."` prints `1`) | typed from the values of the whole result when it is collected (`read_frame`, `read`; `batch::resolve_number_auto_with`, `ColumnKind::NumberAuto`): `Int64` when every non-null value is integral and fits an `i64`; `Float64` when a value has a fraction and every such value survives a round trip through `f64`; otherwise (an integral value beyond `i64`, a fraction an `f64` cannot hold such as `0.78431372549019607843`, or no value at all) the exact `Decimal` as before. The rows of such a result are held until the end so every batch has one schema (no cast, nothing is narrowed). A stream handed out batch by batch (`SqlDriver::read_stream`, one schema for the whole stream) types the column as exact decimal text (`NumericText`, as PostgreSQL's unconstrained `NUMERIC`), whatever later chunks hold. Declared precision / scale (`NUMBER(10, 2)`, `NUMBER(38)`, `INTEGER`) keeps its decoding | Arrow has one type per column and an Arrow stream one schema; v4's per-value `int` / `float` becomes a whole-result decision where the result is collected anyway. The exact-decimal fallback deliberately keeps digits v4's `float` lost (a pipe's `numeric` column of 20 digits stays exact). Tests: `driver_native.rs::oracle_driver` (streamed int-then-float and int-then-huge-int chunk by chunk; collected results spanning two 100000-row chunks), `batch::tests::number_auto_resolves_per_chunk` |
| W17X-001 | `sql <label> "<statement>; <statement>"` (user-supplied SQL through `mrsm sql`, and `SQLConnector.exec`-style paths) with more than one statement. v4.1.4 hands the text to SQLAlchemy in one call, so the driver decides: SQLite (`sqlite3`) raises `ProgrammingError: You can only execute one statement at a time` and runs neither statement; MySQL and MariaDB (`pymysql`) raise error 1064 (`syntax error ... near 'INSERT ...'`) and run neither. PostgreSQL / TimescaleDB (`psycopg`) and MSSQL (`pyodbc`) accept the string, and Oracle rejects it, in v4 and v5 alike. Measured 2026-10-18 with `scripts/transition` (`run_all.py run-<flavor> <flavor> --only ddl`, step `multi-statement` = `CREATE TABLE t (a INT); INSERT INTO t (a) VALUES (5)`, then `SELECT * FROM t` and `DROP TABLE t`) on sqlite, mysql, mariadb, postgis, mssql and oracle: v4 fails on sqlite, mysql and mariadb and the table never exists; the other three behave the same in both | v5 sends the text as written, so both statements run on SQLite, MySQL and MariaDB (sqlx enables multi-statements on MySQL); the other flavors are unchanged. Scope: SQL the user supplies. Internal query building never relies on multi-statement execution (each statement is its own call), and `clean()` still blocks `;` in the internal names it validates | intentional (decided 2026-10-18 with the wave coordinator): executing what the user typed is the less surprising behavior, and a statement list that works on PostgreSQL and MSSQL should not fail on SQLite and MySQL. Evidence: the `scripts/transition` ddl stage classifies steps `multi-statement`, `select multi-statement table`, `drop second table` as EXPECTED W17X-001 on sqlite, mysql and mariadb |
| W17O-010 | a statement that changes data but contains `select` (`INSERT INTO t SELECT * FROM s`, `CREATE VIEW v AS SELECT ...`) reaches `read()` through `sql <label> "<query>"`; v4.1.4 runs it and then raises SQLAlchemy's `ResourceClosedError` (no result set). Run on this host's test containers with v4.1.4 (a scratch venv with `psycopg`, `pymysql`, `oracledb`, `pyodbc`, `duckdb-engine` next to the pinned reference packages), then the table is read back in a separate process. DML (`INSERT ... SELECT`): rows kept on MSSQL only; rolled back on SQLite, PostgreSQL (psycopg), MySQL (pymysql), MariaDB, Oracle and DuckDB (DuckDB printed `Count 1` and kept nothing). DDL (`CREATE VIEW ... AS SELECT`): kept on MSSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, Oracle, SQLite; rolled back on PostgreSQL and DuckDB. The cause is `read()` running inside `with self.engine.begin()` except on MSSQL (`SKIP_READ_TRANSACTION_FLAVORS = ['mssql']`, `_sql.py:31,322-329`): the exception rolls the transaction back, and MySQL / MariaDB / Oracle commit DDL implicitly. The other PostgreSQL-family flavors (TimescaleDB, PostGIS, Citus, CockroachDB) share psycopg and the same code path (not run separately) | v5 runs the statement and commits it on every flavor (SQLite, PostgreSQL, MySQL, MariaDB, MSSQL, Oracle, DuckDB, checked against the same containers with `target/debug/mrsm`); the action prints `None` after "This result object does not return rows" (DuckDB: its `Count` table, as v4) | intentional (maintainer decision 2026-10-18): the user asked for the statement, and v4's silent rollback was a bug that depended on the flavor. Supersedes the DDL-only reading of W17O-007a / W17O-008. Migration guide: "Behavior changes at a glance", `sql <label> "<query>"` that contains `select` but changes data. Tests: `w17b_read_ddl.rs::read_commits_an_insert_select` and `read_runs_a_create_view` (every flavor) |

| W17C-CP-001 | v4 has no shell completions outside its own shell; the shell completes `show c` to `columns`, `config`, `connectors` (fixture `tests/fixtures/shell_completions.json`) | v5 adds `show|install|uninstall completions` (v5-only subactions, `V5_ONLY_SUBACTIONS`), so the interactive shell's `show c` also offers `completions`; the program's own completions (`mrsm show completions <shell>`, `_MRSM_COMPLETE`) reuse `CompletionData::get_completions` and add flags, flag values (`-i`, `-c`, `-r`, `-e`, `choices`, files for `--file` / `--env-file`) and `--flag=value` | additive. The v4 fixture is untouched: `tests/shell_completions.rs::completions_match_v4` filters the one new word out of `show c` (an overlay, with an assertion that it is offered). Limits, by design: pipe keys (`-m`, `-l`) and tags are not completed (they need the instance; the interactive shell does not either); plugin-defined Python completers (`complete_<action>`) are not run (that would start Python): a plugin action completes its subactions; plugin actions, flags and `plugin:` connectors come from the manifest the last command cached, never rebuilt by a completion; `-i` completes `sql`, `api`, `valkey` and plugin instance types (the interactive shell offers every connector for `-i`, `-c` and `-r`), `-r` completes `api:` labels |
| W17C-K2-001 | v4 has no way to update a database's extensions; `upgrade` without (or with an unknown) subaction lists `meerschaum`, `mrsm`, `packages`, `plugins` | v5 adds `upgrade extensions [databases ...] [-i <instance>]` (v5-only subaction, `V5_ONLY_SUBACTIONS`; `crates/meerschaum-cli/src/native/extensions.rs` over `meerschaum_connector_sql::extensions`), so the `upgrade` choices list also shows `upgrade extensions`. It updates every PostgreSQL extension whose installed version lags the server's default (TimescaleDB as the first statement of its own session, the PostGIS family with `postgis_extensions_upgrade()`, the rest with `ALTER EXTENSION ... UPDATE`), the instance's database plus the databases named as words only; on the stack's database (`sql:main` on a local host, a compose file in the root, and a `db` container for the stack's project) as a superuser it also applies `TIMESCALEDB_TELEMETRY: 'off'` (`ALTER SYSTEM`, reload, telemetry job unscheduled) and asks whether to preload `pg_stat_statements` (default no; `-y` / `--force` apply it without asking, `--noask` keeps the default and prints the SQL and `mrsm stack restart db`; user decision 2026-10-18), and restarts the stack's running `api` and `grafana` after a TimescaleDB update | additive. Tests: `crates/meerschaum-connector-sql/tests/w17c_extensions.rs` (incl. the negative test: TimescaleDB refuses the update after a first query), `crates/meerschaum-cli/tests/w17c_upgrade_extensions.rs`, unit tests in both modules; the stack path was checked on a scratch HA pg18 stack |
| W17C-K2-002 | `stack up`, `start api` and `upgrade mrsm` print nothing about the database's extensions | `stack up` (on success) and `start api` (as a startup warning) print ONE line when `sql:main` / the API's PostgreSQL instance answers within 2 s and an extension lags, naming `mrsm upgrade extensions -i <keys>`; `upgrade mrsm`, after it brought the stack up, waits up to 90 s for the `db` container to be healthy (or running, without a healthcheck) and prints the hint once and offers to run `upgrade extensions` (`--yes` runs it, `--noask` only prints the hint). Every probe is read-only, bounded and swallows errors | additive output; `upgrade mrsm` can take up to 90 s longer when the stack's database never becomes healthy |

### Wave 17c (task F2): follow-ups from the private-corpus replays

| ID | v4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-F2-001 | a sync whose plugin calls `SQLConnector.read()` prints nothing at exit (the process ends through `sys.exit`, so `multiprocessing`'s finalizers unlink the named semaphores of the `ThreadPool` `read()` takes from `utils/pool.py`) | the Python worker ended with `os._exit(0)` (`worker/_server.py`), skipping those finalizers; with Python 3.14's default `forkserver` start method the resource tracker then printed `UserWarning: resource_tracker: There appear to be N leaked semaphore objects to clean up at shutdown` after every such sync | Fixed: `release_multiprocessing()` runs before `os._exit` in CPython's order: the higher-priority finalizers (pool teardown) first, then the semaphores' `SemLock._cleanup` (always, it never blocks), then children and the rest; the blocking parts run in a daemon thread sharing a 1.5 s budget (under the host's 5 s grace), and `os._exit` stays so a stuck plugin thread cannot keep the worker alive. Test: `test_worker.py::test_worker_exit_releases_multiprocessing_semaphores` |
| W17C-F2-002 | `register pipe -c <unknown type> -m x` is silent (`Pipe.register()` builds `Pipe.connector` with `warnings.simplefilter('ignore')`, `core/Pipe/__init__.py:603-608`); `PluginConnector` calls any `register` attribute | printed `Cannot create Connector of type 'x'.` from the worker's `get_connector` (22 times in one `compose up`) | Fixed: the worker builds a pipe's connector quietly for `describe_connector` / `fetch` / `sync` / `subscribe` / `register` (`_get_connector(..., quiet=True)`; the suppressed warnings are kept and appended to the error when the connector cannot be built), and `register pipes` asks the worker for a `register()` hook only when the plugin manifest says the plugin or connector type may define one (`manage.rs::manifest_may_register`). The manifest's `data_functions` now list every callable attribute (schema 5), so a `functools.partial` or callable-object `register` is still applied. Tests: `compose_registration.rs` (`register_pipe_of_an_unknown_connector_type_is_silent`, `a_non_function_register_hook_is_applied`), `test_worker.py::test_pipe_connector_ops_build_the_connector_quietly` |
| W17C-F2-003 | `compose up` registers each new pipe with `run_mrsm_command(['register', 'pipes', ...], _subprocess=False)`, i.e. `entry(sysargs)` in-process (`compose/utils/__init__.py:69-72`): the result is returned, never printed; `info()` lines print at once; `install required` goes through `os.listdir(PLUGINS_RESOURCES_PATH)` (`plugins/__init__.py:1010-1018`) | a child `mrsm` per command printed `Successfully registered 1 pipe.` per pipe (and `Installed packages for N plugins` for `install required`); compose's own messages were printed after the whole subaction; `install required` went through the plugins alphabetically | Fixed: in-process `register pipes` runs inside the compose process under the project's config and worker environment (`compose_action.rs::MrsmRunner::run_in_process`; a project `environment:` `MRSM_SQL_<LABEL>` wins over the host's, test `compose_registration_uses_the_projects_connector_environment`); every other in-process command runs as `mrsm __mrsm_entry_child__ <result file> <sysargs>` with the `MRSM__ENTRY_CHILD` marker (taken out of the environment at start, so jobs never inherit it), and writes its `(success, message)` into a 0600 file in a private 0700 directory the parent creates exclusively and removes; compose's messages are shown before each command; `install required` keeps the order of the plugin links directory. A synthetic 70-pipe `compose up --dry` prints what v4.1.4 prints, ANSI stripped. Remaining difference: a `register()` that raises is printed from the worker, with the worker's frames instead of `core/Pipe/_register.py:59`; it is a rich traceback as in v4 when `rich` is importable in the worker (else a plain one), and it goes to stderr, not stdout: a worker's stdout is its stderr by design (`crates/meerschaum-worker/python/host.py:13`, a worker must never write into an MCP host's protocol stdout) |
| W17C-F2-004 | `compose up --dry` on a 71-pipe file: 4.7 s | 20.5 s: one child `mrsm` per new pipe, each starting a Python worker for the connector's `register()` hook (even for connector types no plugin defines) | Fixed by W17C-F2-002/003: one worker per project. Synthetic 70 pipes (30 SQL, 20 unknown type, 10 + 10 plugin pipes with and without `register()`, plugins taking 0.4 s to import): before 21.6 s, after 2.5 s (debug binary), v4.1.4 5.9 s. Guard: `compose_registration.rs` (111 pipes: at most 6 Python processes, was 14 for 11 Python-backed pipes) |
| W17C-F2-005 | `sync pipes --loop --min-seconds 300` prints `Sleeping for 300.0 seconds.` (`actions/sync.py:414`, `--min-seconds` is `type=float`; the default `min_seconds: int = 1` prints `1 second.`) | the engine's lap message printed the `f64` as `300` | Fixed in the CLI (`native/sync.rs::sleeping_message`): the parsed argument is printed as v4 formats it; a sleep shortened by a pipe's `schedule` (v5-only) keeps the engine's text |
| W17C-F2-006 | (v5-only) `plan pipes` | a provisional node (`*`) was printed with the static plan's window (e.g. `skip*`, or `incremental* [12:00, 13:21)`), while the executor re-plans it after its parents sync and usually runs a `backfill` from the start of the parent's grid cell (`[2026-10-04 00:00, …)` on the 30-day grid, §5.4), which is what the plugin's `fetch` received | `plan pipes` (table and `--nopretty` JSON) and `Pipeline.plan(expected=True)` return `plan::expected`: each provisional node re-planned as the executor would, assuming every node that runs before it writes new rows from its watermark on (reason prefixed `if its parents sync new rows:`, or `no parent is expected to sync:`). Only the window's start is predicted; its end is the parents' current watermarks (the executor caps at their new ones). `Pipeline.plan()` (default `expected=False`), the engine API and the golden fixtures keep the static plan. `--json` is not a Meerschaum flag (v4 has none); `--nopretty` is the machine-readable mode of `plan pipes`, `show graph` and `show runs`. Tests: `engine_views.rs::plan_pipes_shows_the_window_the_executor_passes`, `test_pipeline.py::test_cli_plan_is_pipeline_plan_expected` |
| W17C-F2-007 | (v5-only) `/dash/<plugin>/` served by the extension's `mrsm` vs the standalone binary | the replay saw 6983 B vs 12921 B for one private plugin page | Not a code difference: with a synthetic Dash plugin (`tests/plugins/w7_dashapp`) both modes serve byte-identical pages apart from Dash's random per-process `end_id`, the same `_dash-layout` and `_dash-dependencies`. The replay's extension run auto-installed `dash_ace` into the shared `mrsm` venv mid-request (its log shows the install from `meerschaum/api/dash/components.py:21`); the standalone run came after it with the same `MRSM_VENVS_DIR`, so the two page loads saw different component libraries. Environmental |

### Wave 17c (task CI1): newest FastAPI, connectors with explicit attributes in in-process actions

| ID | v4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-CI1-001 | `meerschaum/api/__init__.py` builds `fastapi.FastAPI(..., open_api_tags=[...])` (a misspelling of `openapi_tags`); FastAPI up to 0.14x swallowed unknown keyword arguments, so the API's OpenAPI document never carried the tag descriptions | FastAPI 0.146 rejects the keyword (`TypeError: FastAPI.__init__() got an unexpected keyword argument 'open_api_tags'`), so `import meerschaum.api` failed and the API host (every `@api_plugin` route, plugin MCP tool and plugin Dash app) never started | Fixed: the host passes `openapi_tags`, so the host's own OpenAPI document (`/openapi.json` on the host socket) lists the tag descriptions v4 intended. The Rust server's public `/openapi.json` is its own document (`meerschaum-server/src/openapi.rs`) and is unchanged. Test: `tests/api_host/test_host_flags.py::test_the_host_app_builds_with_its_openapi_tags`. The test environment installs `httpx2` next to `httpx` (Starlette 1.9's `starlette.testclient` deprecates `httpx` and falls back to it with a `StarletteDeprecationWarning`); neither is a runtime dependency of the host, so no extra changed |
| W17C-CI1-002 | an action run in the process (`actions['compress'](['pipes'], mrsm_instance='sql:x')`, `mrsm.entry(...)`) resolves `sql:x` through `get_connector('sql', 'x')`, which returns the connector the process already built, e.g. with `flavor='timescaledb-ha'`, even when `MRSM_SQL_X=postgresql://...` also defines `sql:x` | `adhoc_context()` (`meerschaum/_internal/entry.py`) handed the native dispatcher only connectors the config registry and the environment did not define, so the dispatcher rebuilt `sql:x` from the URI alone: the flavor silently became `postgresql` and `compress pipes` failed with "Compression is not supported for flavor 'postgresql'" (pytest CI, `test_maintenance_actions[timescaledb_ha]`, where `scripts/test.sh` exports the test connectors' URIs) | Fixed in the façade: `Connector._set_attributes` records the keyword arguments the caller passed (`_explicit_attributes`, not part of `meta`), and a configured connector whose explicit attributes the configured one lacks or sets differently is passed to the dispatcher, so the process's connector wins as in v4 (a connector rebuilt with new keyword arguments replaces the old one there too). Connectors built from the config alone are still left to the dispatcher. Tests: `tests/test_adhoc_connectors.py` (env URI with a `postgresql` scheme plus a registered `timescaledb-ha` connector under the same label; an explicit `uri` over an env URI; a rebuilt connector) and `tests/test_maintenance.py` with the URIs `scripts/test.sh` exports |

### Wave 17c (task PG): `upgrade postgres`, `backup postgres`, `restore postgres`

| ID | v4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-PG-001 | v4 cannot move the stack's database to another PostgreSQL major: a newer image in `stack.yaml` cannot start on the old data directory, and the stack's images ship one major each (no `pg_upgrade`); `upgrade` without (or with an unknown) subaction lists `meerschaum`, `mrsm`, `packages`, `plugins` | v5 adds `upgrade postgres [<major> \| <image> \| rollback]` (v5-only subaction, `V5_ONLY_SUBACTIONS`; `crates/meerschaum-cli/src/native/upgrade_postgres/`), so the `upgrade` choices list and `upgrade p<TAB>` also offer `postgres`. Through the stack's own Compose project and the client tools inside containers: a read-only preflight (a throwaway container of the target image creates every extension and each database's exact TimescaleDB version), the services that depend on `db` stopped, a full backup (W17C-PG-002's dump, exact row counts in each dump's snapshot, the new image's `pg_dump` when it reaches the old server), a NEW volume set up by the target image in a network-less temporary container, TimescaleDB created at the dump's version, `timescaledb_pre_restore()` / `pg_restore --exit-on-error` with a filtered list / `timescaledb_post_restore()`, `ALTER SYSTEM` settings carried over (list settings one literal per element; the server restarted to prove it starts), verification of exact row counts and structure (old vs new, and the old database before vs after), extensions updated, then `stack.yaml` copied byte for byte and only the `db` image, its data volume and that volume's declaration edited. The old volume is never removed; `rollback` restores the copy and the old volume; any failure or Ctrl-C puts the old stack back. A `db` that cannot start (its image newer than the data, read from the volume's `PG_VERSION`) gets the exact image to pin first | additive. Tests: `upgrade_postgres::*` unit tests (image mapping, restore-list filter, globals rewrite, `--create` script split, `postgresql.auto.conf` parsing, list settings, config edit, fingerprint comparison, retention, run directories), `tests/test_upgrade_postgres.py` (`MRSM_TEST_DOCKER=1`: HA pg17 -> pg18 with a v4-era `stack.yaml`, rollback, an existing new volume refused, failure injection; `timescale/timescaledb:latest-pg17` -> `latest-pg18` with the moved `PGDATA` and a carried `shared_preload_libraries`); the `upgrade p` overlay in `tests/shell_completions.rs` |
| W17C-PG-002 | v4 has no backup of the stack's database (v4's `BACKUP_RESOURCES_PATH`, `<root>/backup`, is never written) | `backup postgres` (v5-only action `backup`, `V5_ONLY_ACTIONS`): an ONLINE backup (nothing stopped): `pg_dumpall --globals-only` and, per database, `pg_dump -Fc --create` (zstd when the server's `pg_dump` supports it) of a snapshot a session exports (`pg_export_snapshot()`, so the structure fingerprint and row estimate recorded with it describe exactly the dump), into `<root>/backups/postgres/<UTC timestamp>.partial/`, renamed when every dump was hashed (SHA-256) and read back with `pg_restore --list`; 0700 directories, 0600 files; a `manifest.json` with the server version, image, extensions per database, sizes, checksums and row estimates; the optional `system:backups:postgres:keep` (not in the defaults) prunes only complete backups. It asks nothing (scheduled with `-s 'daily' -d`) | additive. Tests: `tests/test_upgrade_postgres.py::test_backup_restore_rollback_and_retention_on_a_running_stack` (container IDs and start times unchanged, permissions, checksums, `--noask`, retention keeps the restore's directory) |
| W17C-PG-003 | no restore of the stack's database | `restore postgres [<timestamp> \| <path> \| rollback]` (v5-only action `restore`): checksums checked first (a damaged backup is refused before anything changes), a throwaway container of the stack's image lists every dump and creates every extension and TimescaleDB version, then W17C-PG-001's restore into a NEW volume, verification against the manifest (structure; exact counts when the backup recorded them), and the switch of the `db` volume only; `restore postgres rollback` and `upgrade postgres rollback` both switch back to the latest replaced volume. An older major's backup is restored the way an upgrade restores its dump; a newer major's is refused | additive. Same tests (restore equals the backup's state, rollback equals the changed state, damaged backup refused) |

### Wave 17c (task CI1, review fixes)

| ID | v4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-CI1-003 | `mrsm.entry([...])` never touched a connector the action did not use | `adhoc_context()` built the Rust connector of every ad hoc SQL connector with `_core_connector()`, which asks an unconfigured-schema database for its default schema; an unreachable one made EVERY `mrsm.entry()` wait for the pool's 30 s acquire timeout (the failed discovery was retried each call). W17C-CI1-002 made configured connectors with explicit attributes reach this path too | Fixed: `SQLConnector._core_connector(discover_schema=False)` returns the lazily connecting Rust connector without asking anything; its `InstanceConnector` methods still ask for the default schema before they run (`SqlConnector::require_schema`), so an action that uses the connector behaves as before, and one that does not costs nothing (`mrsm.entry(['show', 'version'])` with an unreachable explicit connector: 30.0 s -> ~0.1 s). An explicit attribute whose `==` raises or has no single truth value (an array) counts as differing instead of crashing `entry()`. Test: `tests/test_adhoc_connectors.py::test_an_unreachable_explicit_connector_does_not_slow_entry`. Test isolation (no product change): the engine tests on SQLite (`tests/test_pipeline.py`, `tests/test_pipeline_state.py`) use a SQLite file of their own per module and `pytest -n` worker (`tests/_worker_sqlite.py`) instead of the shared `test_sqlite.db`, where other workers' writes made a chain sync fail with `database is locked` after SQLite's 5 s busy timeout (the same timeout and rollback journal as v4's `sqlite3`, kept); the API host test helpers filter Starlette 1.9's `httpx` deprecation warning so they pass with or without `httpx2` |

### Wave 17c (task GEO): geometry SRID and spatial reference parity

| ID | v4.1.4 | Rust | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-GEO-001 | `filter_unseen_df` builds its delta through a pandas merge (`utils/dataframe.py`), which drops the geometry columns' CRS; without an old frame it returns the input unchanged. On PostGIS / TimescaleDB-HA, a bare `geometry` pipe therefore stores SRID 0 on both inserted and updated rows, even from a GeoDataFrame with a CRS | The kernel retained the frame CRS on the delta, so the update staging table acquired that SRID and updated rows differed from inserted rows | Fixed: `kernel::diff::filter_unseen_df` clears the result columns' CRS when comparing against an old frame. Typed `geometry[Point, 4326]` columns keep SRID 4326 through their PostGIS typmod. Geometry on `postgresql` / `timescaledb` remains plain hex WKB TEXT with SRID 0, as in v4, even when the server has PostGIS installed. Tests: `w17c_geometry_crs.rs`, `w17c_geometry_srid.rs`, `tests/test_geometry_srid.py` |
| W17C-GEO-002 | GeoPackage `_init_geopackage_pipe` writes `df.head(0).to_file(..., driver='GPKG')` (`connectors/sql/_pipes.py`), so `gpkg_geometry_columns.srs_id` comes from the frame's geometry CRS. A bare `geometry` dtype with an EPSG:4326 GeoDataFrame registers 4326 | Metadata used only the configured dtype SRID and registered the undefined 99999 for that frame | Fixed: `init_geopackage_pipe` reads the geometry column's frame CRS before falling back to the dtype SRID. Geometry blob headers still carry -1 (`serialize_geometry(..., 'gpkg_wkb')` uses `srid or -1` in v4). Tests: `w17c_geometry_srid.rs::geopackage_srs`, `tests/test_geometry_srid.py::test_geopackage_registers_frame_crs` |
| W17C-GEO-003 | On PostGIS / TimescaleDB-HA the second sync of `geography[Point, 4326]` changes the column to TEXT (`get_alter_columns_queries`, `connectors/sql/_pipes.py`): updated and newly inserted rows carry SRID-less WKB, while untouched rows keep the geography's EWKB | The same column conversion occurred, but the retained delta CRS gave updated rows EWKB with SRID 4326 | Fixed by W17C-GEO-001; the inherited v4 geography-to-TEXT behavior is retained. Test: `w17c_geometry_srid.rs::postgis_family` |


### Wave 17c PostgreSQL safety follow-up

| ID | Trigger / reference | v5 handling | Evidence / status |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-PG-004 | Upgrade safety review: a write after a dump can leave counts unchanged; volume names can collide with the live volume | Compare per-database tuple activity and structure before stopping the old database immediately after the dumps, then restore; refuse changes even with `--force`. Choose unused run-specific volume names on collision, prove the run label after creation, and remove only that run's volumes. This supersedes W17C-PG-001's existing-volume refusal and switch-only database downtime description | Docker regression tests cover a post-dump UPDATE, a live volume named for the target major, a retained rollback volume, and restore verification failure |
| W17C-PG-005 | Upgrade / restore / rollback can act on a Compose project belonging to another root or trust a manifest's config path | Require Compose origin labels for this root; lock the root and project on the Docker daemon; validate rollback config paths. Unix SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP request cleanup, further signals are shielded, and interrupted runs are recovered before later work. Preserve locale/encoding and non-public TimescaleDB schemas; isolate auxiliary mounts and refuse unsupported volume drivers | Unit coverage plus Docker regressions for root ownership, changed manifest paths, locale, schema and SIGHUP / repeated SIGINT / forced-kill recovery; Linux/macOS mode bits, checksum limitations and config rewrite fallback documented |
| W17C-PG-006 | v4 `stack up` forwards `--force` to Compose and does not check the existing data major | Before startup, compare named-volume `PG_VERSION` with the configured known image's major through a read-only, no-pull probe; refuse mismatch with pin/upgrade guidance. Skip a matching running image. `stack up --force` bypasses the check and is consumed by Meerschaum | Intentional additive safety behavior; isolated host unit tests and stopped-cluster Docker regression. Unknown image families and tags without a major cannot be checked |
| W17C-PG-007 | Adversarial follow-up: a new client could write between the final check and server stop; a forced interruption could leave rollback or network recovery invisible | Fence external writers by journalling and disconnecting all supported Docker network attachments before dumping, retain isolation through verified server stop, then reconnect aliases/static IPAM. Refuse standby sources; refuse enabled subscriptions and unhandled write workers by default. Journal rollback before mutations; interrupted rollback completes the requested move while keeping both verified volumes. Incomplete recovery remains nonterminal. Recovery-critical files and manifest directory entries are synced on Unix before proceeding | All 15 rebuilt Docker scenarios passed, including the deterministic external SQL attempt after the last check and kill-after-rollback-config-edit tests; source and execution evidence independently accepted. Administrator writes inside the database container / through its Unix socket must be stopped separately; force warnings explicitly describe concurrent-write risk |
| W17C-K2-003 | Safety review of extension tuning: `sql:main` plus localhost and a project-labeled container do not prove stack ownership or endpoint | Supersedes K2-001's classification shorthand: require current-root Compose origin, correct project/service/running container, database and published/internal port, cluster identity and container server address, then live PID plus random application_name proof observed inside that owned container. This distinguishes remote/local physical clones with identical static identity/IP. Recheck DB before restarts and require each API/Grafana service's origin too. Explicit extension updates still work when stack classification fails; stack server tuning/restarts are omitted | Four focused unit regressions cover foreign roots/services, wrong port/database/ID/address, internal/IPv6 bindings and identical-static-identity clones without the live nonce. Independent source review ready; coordinated compiled validation pending |

### Wave 17c DuckDB transport follow-up

| ID | Trigger / reference | v5 handling | Evidence / status |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-DUCK-001 | v4 Pipe and SQLAlchemy share DuckDB's public Python instance; independent native/Python instances can acknowledge writes that disappear on close | In Python hosts, lazily route DuckDB driver I/O through the installed public driver while preserving Rust Pipe/query algorithms. Share canonical file anchors and per-object memory anchors, with duplicate independent connections and configured memory aliases. Standalone and base-only installs keep the C API | `tests/test_duckdb_transport.py` covers alternating persisted writes in both open/close orders, held SQLAlchemy transactions, memory isolation and registry routing; all 28 transport/base cases pass on the rebuilt debug extension, including all four persisted-write combinations; optimized release gate remains pending |
| W17C-DUCK-002 | Mixed driver selections, changed per-file settings, inherited handles, or custom connection routing can open incompatible DuckDB snapshots | Explicit `MRSM_DUCKDB_LIB` pins C API and refuses SDK SQLAlchemy access; late driver/config changes, unsupported creator/module/do_connect routing, and fork reuse fail closed. Public-driver minimums are duckdb 1.5.6 / duckdb-engine 0.17.0; selection never installs or upgrades optional packages. Raw SQL first retains optional installation behavior | Intentional connection safety constraints, documented in migration/design/crate docs. Policy, configuration, exact type conversion and managed fork cleanup pass in the 28-case transport/base suite; native cancellation / abandoned-stream units pass. Parameterized native reads require one SELECT and bind metadata without evaluating the data plan; use SQLConnector.exec for writes or multiple statements |

| W17C-DUCK-003 | Shared frame scalar extraction wrapped UInt64 / Int128 outside i64, including UBIGINT returned as -1 in `as_docs` | Keep in-range Int values and carry overflow through the existing exact Decimal representation; rebuild UInt64 / Int128 with checked exact integers. `as_docs` restores Python int from the original native integer dtype while explicit DECIMAL stays Decimal | Kernel boundary/null/reconstruction/rejection regression and SDK unsigned type/value regression; full kernel suite 46 passed; rebuilt SDK suite 28 passed, including Pipe / connector docs and chunk iteration with exact integer values and types alongside explicit DECIMAL |

### Wave 17c doctor / DuckDB integration

| ID | Trigger / reference | v5 handling | Evidence / status |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17C-DR-001 | The new Python DuckDB transport does not need libduckdb, but doctor still required the native library and suggested an override that disables SQLAlchemy sharing | A read-only host callback reports existing transport policy and optional driver metadata without importing DuckDB, selecting a policy, loading a native library or opening a database. Python-host live instance checks explicitly SKIP; driver metadata is compared with the compiled extra requirement. Covers metadata-only PASS/FAIL/WARN, no optional import/database/cache creation, unchanged policy and fail-closed inherited/changed policy |

The existing per-step guard retains cached-only diagnostics and restores normal discovery for later actions. Standalone startup ownership is local to each Runtime and released on early parse/help/version returns. The action facade enters the guard after its explicit registry import, which independently discovers plugins | Rebuilt g24 dispatch2 and CLI integration25 passed; SDK doctor16 passed4.79s with zero skips. Plugin/cache sentinels cover direct / facade / patched / malformed / overflow doctor and normal loading after chains or reversed patches; facade setup adds a new plugin and discovery tripwire after the explicit registry import |

### Wave 17 transition CLI display differences

| ID | Before | Now | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| W17T-CLI-001 | `show data` of a `bytes` column prints the decoded bytes raw through tabulate: control characters reach the terminal, and leading/trailing whitespace bytes (`\t \n \v \f \r`) are silently dropped, so displayed values are wrong (`b'\n\x01\x02'` shows as `\x01\x02`) | prints each value's `repr` (`b'\n\x01\x02'`) | v4's rendering is lossy and terminal-unsafe; data returned by `get_data()` is identical (transition `pipes` checks) |
| W17T-CLI-002 | `show tokens` leaves the User column blank | shows the owning username | v4 does not resolve the token's user for the table; the token IDs, labels, expirations and validity are identical |
| W17T-CLI-003 | `show connectors` "Active connectors" prints the in-process registry of a fresh CLI: the built-in type keys with empty dicts (`{'api': {}, 'sql': {}, 'plugin': {}, 'valkey': {}}`) | `{}` | the Rust CLI has no Python connector registry to print; cosmetic only. Kept for rc1 |
| W17T-CLI-004 | `show plugins` lists plugins in file-system order | sorted by name | as W13I-008 (cross-reference so the transition's EXPECTED carries a W17 anchor) |

### Wave 18 job startup stagger (D23)

| ID | v4 behavior | v5 handling | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18-JB-001 | A job's supervisor starts its worker at once, every time: `compose up`, `restart jobs`, a reboot bringing every systemd unit up, all start every job together. A unit has no `RestartSec=` (systemd's 100 ms default). | Every job passes a per-root startup admission gate before its worker starts (`jobs.startup.*`: random jitter in `[0, 30]` s, wait while the 1-minute load average is above `ncpu * 1.5` or less than 10% of memory is available, then one of `max(2, ncpu/2)` file-lock slots under `<root>/jobs/.startup/`, held until the first lap is done, the worker exits, the worker and its children stay under 10% of one core for `settle_seconds` (15; Linux) or 300 s pass). Bulk and automatic starts (`start jobs` with several jobs, `restart jobs`, compose, unit restarts) get all of it; an explicit `start job x` only the slot; `--no-stagger` none. Units get `RestartSec=5`. | `crates/meerschaum-jobs/src/startup.rs`; tests `tests/startup.rs`, `tests/layout.rs` and `tests/systemd_executor.rs` overlay `RestartSec=5` on the v4 unit text. A job waiting at the gate reads as running and has no `process.began` yet. |
| W18-JB-002 | (D23 asked for `RandomizedDelaySec=<jitter>` in generated units.) | Not emitted: `RandomizedDelaySec` is a `[Timer]` option; systemd 259 logs `Unknown key 'RandomizedDelaySec' in section [Service], ignoring` for it in a service. The unit's `ExecStart` is the job wrapper, which applies the same random delay (`[0, jitter_seconds]`) on every start and restart, so the effect is the one asked for. | `systemd-analyze --user verify` on a unit carrying the key. |
| W18-JB-003 | `-s` has no jitter; the word `jitter` after a schedule is an invalid frequency. | `-s 'every 5 minutes jitter 30 seconds'`, `--jitter 30s` (with a schedule) and `jobs.schedule.jitter_seconds` (default 0) move every fire time by a FIXED per-job offset in `[0, n]` s (FNV-1a of the job's name, so it is the same on every fire and restart). `--startup-jitter 30s` and `--no-stagger` are the matching start flags. | Additive; `crates/meerschaum-jobs/tests/schedule_jitter.rs`. |

### Wave 18 shell, terminal and prompts (decisions 1-5)

| ID | v4 behavior | v5 handling | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18-SH-001 | Ctrl-C during an action raises `KeyboardInterrupt`; in rc1 the spinner guard re-raised SIGINT, so Ctrl-C killed the whole process, shell included | The watcher thread (`shell/interrupt.rs`) turns SIGINT into a cooperative cancellation: `Session::block_on` drops its future and unwinds with a silent payload which the dispatcher turns into `Interrupted.`; the shell returns to its prompt, `mrsm <action>` exits 130. Actions on the stop flag (`install_stop_handler`) finish the current step; a second Ctrl-C ends `mrsm <action>` at once and cancels a shell command after two seconds. Programs and worker calls keep the terminal's Ctrl-C (a second one within 2 s kills them). Job workers keep the stop-flag semantics | `tests/interrupt_shell.rs` (PTY), unit tests in `shell/interrupt.rs`; embedded hosts (`entry()`), servers and jobs are unchanged. SIGKILL of a program reaches its pid only, not its own children |
| W18-SH-002 | A password prompt echoes nothing (`prompt_toolkit` `is_password=True`) | Echoes `*` per character (Backspace erases one, Ctrl-U all, Ctrl-C cancels); nothing at all when stdin is not a terminal. `bootstrap connectors` asks secret attributes (`password`, `api_key`, `secret`, `token`, `client_secret`, ... by name, `is_secret_attribute`) the same way | `native/terminal.rs` `masked_from_events` tests, `tests/prompts_pty.rs`; the wizard's confirmation printout of the new attributes shows secrets as `********` (v4 printed them). `register users` / `edit users` passwords were read with echo in rc1 (`prompt()` is not a secret prompt) and now go through the same masked prompt |
| W18-SH-003 | `mrsm python` starts the REPL whose first line is `import meerschaum as mrsm`, which fails with a traceback in an interpreter without the package | Probes `import meerschaum` first (only when the script would import it: no `--venv`, no code arguments) and fails with `The meerschaum Python package is not installed for <interpreter>; install it with pip install meerschaum==<version> to use mrsm python` | no auto-install, no plain-REPL fallback (decision 3); `tests/actions.rs` |
| W18-SH-004 | `bootstrap pipes` asks the connector with `choose` (a printed list, unknown keys refused) and the metric and location without suggestions | A filtering dropdown (`shell/suggest.rs`): connectors (configuration, `MRSM_<TYPE>_<LABEL>`, `plugin:<name>`, `New`), then metrics and locations already registered on the instance. Unknown connector keys are accepted with a warning | decision 4; the dropdown needs a terminal, otherwise the questions read plain lines |
| W18-SH-005 | `mrsm --version` / `--help` load the plugin manifest first (v5 started a Python worker and, with an old `meerschaum` in the user site, printed a version-mismatch warning) | Both are answered before the manifest is validated; `--help` reads the cached manifest (the plugin actions the last command recorded) | `tests/version_help_no_worker.rs`; a plugin never loaded by any earlier command has no action help until one has run |

| W18-DR-001 | v5 rc1 rendered the web console in Rust (askama + HTMX) and the host mounted per-plugin Dash apps at `/dash/<plugin>/` | The console is v4's Plotly Dash app again (D21), served by the Python API host at the v4 paths; the Rust server deletes its console pages and proxies `/dash` and `/dash/*` to the host when it reports `console.mounted` (`GET /_mrsm/host/status`), else serves a static pointer page ("The web console comes with the Python package: pip install meerschaum[dash]"). The standalone binary and the `minimal` image have no console, only the pointer page. The host starts for the console whenever `dash`, `dash_bootstrap_components` and `a2wsgi` import (a 30 ms probe at `start api`; a new `console` reason next to the plugin reasons) | `/dash/assets/` is Dash's: the terminal's xterm bundle moved to `/webterm/assets/`; `/dash/job-logs/<name>` became `GET /logs/<name>/stream` (SSE, bearer or session cookie); `POST /execute` (new, additive) runs an action line as an ephemeral job for the dashboard's Execute console; native plugin pages are served as fragments at `/_mrsm/native-pages`; on Windows the host listens on TCP loopback with a per-start token (`X-Mrsm-Host-Proxy-Token`) instead of a Unix socket | `crates/meerschaum-server/tests/console.rs`, `crates/meerschaum-web/tests/web.rs`, `crates/meerschaum-cli/tests/console_mount.rs` |

### Wave 18 (task PK): packaging, stack, CI

| ID | v4 | v5 | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18PK-001 | the stack's `api` service runs `bmeares/meerschaum:api` (`config/stack/__init__.py:216`, `default_config.json`) | `bmeares/meerschaum:<version>-full`, `<version>` being this release in PEP 440 spelling (`5.0.0rc2`): the Rust defaults write the marker `{__MRSM_VERSION__}` (`meerschaum_core::config::VERSION_MARKER`, resolved by `default_config` from `python_guard::expected_version`) and the façade's `config/stack/__init__.py` spells the same string from `_version.py` (`tests/test_stack_setup.py`, `crates/meerschaum-core/tests/stack_image.rs`) | a pre-release never moves the floating `api` tag (`buildx.sh`: only version-qualified tags), so a release candidate's stack pulled the v4 API image; the client and the server it starts must be one version, and the `full` image carries API and Dash plugins. A root without `config/stack.yaml` follows every upgrade (the next `stack up -d` recreates only `api`); a `stack.yaml` with a `docker-compose.yaml` section keeps its own image (defaults fill in only a missing key). The parity tests read the v5 value as v4's (`tests/common::v4_stack_text`, `test_yaml.py::_v4_stack`, no fixture edited) |

### Wave 18 show actions, colors and `--nopretty` (decisions 6-10)

| ID | v4 behavior | v5 handling | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18-SW-001 | `show data` prints every row, column and cell (`format_dataframe`, a Markdown pipe table; v4 never truncated) | Pretty mode only: more than `formatting:dataframe:max_rows` (60) rows show `min_rows` (10) of them, head and tail around a `…` row; columns that do not fit the terminal (`$COLUMNS` or the terminal of stdout; a pipe or file gets every column) are replaced by one `…` column; a cell over `max_colwidth` (50) characters ends in `…`; the footer is `[N rows x M columns (showing K rows x J columns)]` with the true size. Only the rows shown are read: the window's row count, then the first and last rows by the datetime axis (a pipe without one reads its first rows and the count). `--all` (parsed for `show data` only, `parse::engine_arguments`) and `--nopretty` print everything as v4 did. The keys have no default entry in `default_config.json` (no config-parity change); absent keys never warn or write a file | decision 6; `format/dataframe.rs` (7 unit tests), `native/show.rs` `limited_read_tests` (5: a long window issues only `LIMIT`ed reads, orientation of a backtrack window, no datetime axis, short and missing tables read whole), `tests/w18_show.rs`, `tests/test_show_truncation.py`. A 2M-row PostgreSQL table: 0.2 s against 31 s for the full read |
| W18-SW-002 | `show columns` and `show data` read pipe after pipe (`show rowcounts`, `show sizes` and `show partitions` already used a pool) | `show columns` and `show data` read concurrently with the same `metadata::ordered` / `metadata::concurrency` (`--workers`, default 4, bounded by the connector's pool, serial for DuckDB, in-memory SQLite and plugin connectors) with a progress line, and print in the order of the selection. Whole-table reads (`--all`, `--nopretty`) go a `--workers`-sized batch at a time so memory stays bounded. `clear`, `drop`, `delete` and `verify --yes` stay sequential | decision 7; `tests/w18_show.rs` (`per_pipe_views_print_in_the_order_of_the_pipes_with_workers`: serial and concurrent output are equal), checked on PostgreSQL (`--workers 1` and `4` byte-identical for `columns`, `data`, `sizes`, `rowcounts`). `show indices` and `show schemas` do not exist in v4 or v5 |
| W18-SW-003 | `show sizes` sums every pipe's size (pipes whose `target` is the same table are counted once per pipe) | Each physical table (flavor + host + port + database + schema + resolved target, never credentials; a non-SQL instance by connector keys and target) counts once in the total; the rows of pipes that share one end in `shared xN` (`--nopretty`: an extra tab-separated field on those rows) | decision 8; `tests/w18_show.rs` |
| W18-SW-004 | Colors: `rich` on a forced-terminal console, off only for `NO_COLOR` and `formatting:ansi: false`; piped output stays colored | Unchanged (audit below): every compared native action printed the same bytes as v4.1.4. Where the decision text says "non-TTY stdout" is off "as v4" it is v4 that colors a pipe (checked: `show version \| cat` is magenta), so v5 keeps coloring a pipe. `formatting:ansi: false` keeps rich's bold header SGR in tables as v4 does; `show tokens` and `show targets` print `<rich.table.Table object at 0x...>` in v4 with `ansi: false` (a v4 bug) and the table in v5 | decision 9; the wave 18 SW audit: about 190 command lines compared with `.venv-ref` (piped and in a PTY, with `NO_COLOR` and with `ansi: false`) |
| W18-SW-005 | `--nopretty` exits 0 whatever the command returned (v4 sets the status only when it prints the result tuple) | Exits 1 when the result is a failure (nothing extra is printed, as in v4), 0 otherwise. `doctor --nopretty` follows | decision 10; `tests/w18_show.rs` (`nopretty_exits_nonzero_when_the_command_fails`), `tests/test_show_truncation.py`. Scripts that relied on exit 0 after a failing `--nopretty` command must read the output or `|| true` |

### Wave 18 compose (decisions 11-18, design decision D22)

| ID | v4 behavior | v5 handling | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18-CO-001 | `compose explain` initializes the root directory (`show version`, "Initializing Meerschaum root directory", `install required` in a fresh root) and writes `.compose-cache.pkl` | `explain` creates nothing, writes no cache, installs nothing, fetches no plugin; it reports what `up` would install (`pending-plugin` finding) | Decision 12. `crates/meerschaum-compose/tests/parity.rs` filters the `show version` / `install required` commands and the two messages from the recorded `explain` runs; `tests/tree.rs::explain_is_side_effect_free` |
| W18-CO-002 | An unresolved `'{type:label}'` host reference resolves to `None` and `up` raises `TypeError: meerschaum.connectors.get_connector() argument after ** must be a mapping, not NoneType` | `explain` lists it (and every unresolved connector, instance, plugin, `MRSM{}` symlink, `{{ Pipe() }}` reference) as a finding and keeps going; `up`, `run` and `sync pipes`/`verify pipes` fail with `connector 'mqtt:x' is not defined on this host (referenced by config.meerschaum.connectors.mqtt.x)`. A missing plugin is a finding only (entry-point plugins and plugin-registered connectors cannot be told from the plugins directories), a configured-type (`sql`, `api`, `valkey`) connector or instance the host does not define blocks | Decision 11. `tests/tree.rs`, `crates/meerschaum-cli/tests/w18_compose.rs` |
| W18-CO-003 | `explain` on an unreachable instance raises `Exception('\n 🛑 ...')` | `explain` reports the instance as a finding and prints the pipes as `Unknown (instance unreachable)`; `up` still raises | Decision 11 ("explain keeps going"); `w9_compose_instance.rs::an_unreachable_instance_raises_like_v4` |
| W18-CO-004 | `-f` is `--force` only; flags before the subaction (`compose --file F up`) lose the subaction | `--file`, `--compose-file`, `-f <x>.yaml` work before or after the subaction; a bare `-f` is still force / follow logs | Decision 14; `compose_action.rs::compose_words` |
| W18-CO-005 | `compose init -c/-m/-l/-t/-i` ignores the filters | Adds the matching registered pipes (with their registered parameters) after two confirmations, as v4's source intended | Decision 15 |
| W18-CO-006 | no `include:` / `parent:` | Additive keys (D22): trees of compose files, root wins, `--project`, `--standalone`, `MRSM__COMPOSE_ROOT` job label with the `--force` guard, per-project config cache file `.compose-cache.<project>.pkl` | Decisions 16-17; `tests/tree.rs` |
| W18-CO-007 | `compose up` starts jobs one `start` at a time | Each job start carries `MRSM_JOB_START_KIND=bulk` (`none` with `--no-stagger`) so the startup gate (D23) applies; `daemon: {jitter, startup_jitter}` map form becomes `--jitter` / `--startup-jitter` flags (not part of a job's identity) | Decision 19-22 (with JB) |

| W18-FX2-001 | a worker start waited out a 60 s + 60 s handshake, and every refused or failing start added a pool backoff (250 ms doubling to 30 s) that the next caller slept through: `compose up --dry` with a stale v4 `meerschaum` in the user site waited 500 s | A start preflights its interpreter (one `python -P -c` that reads files, 10 s limit, once per interpreter per program) and fails with ONE message naming the interpreter, the `meerschaum` found (version, path) or its absence, and the fix (`pip install 'meerschaum==<version>'` into that interpreter, or `MRSM_PYTHON`); the refusal is remembered (10 s doubling to 5 min, until `reload`) so later callers get it at once, with no backoff; the connect + `hello` handshake has one 20 s deadline (`MRSM_WORKER_STARTUP_TIMEOUT`) and a silent worker is killed and refused with the same kind of message | `crates/meerschaum-worker/tests/preflight.rs` |

### Wave 18 (task PF): performance (decision 38)

| ID | v4 / rc1 behavior | v5 handling | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18-PF-001 | rc1 (and row 22 above) dropped the Python pipe's cached `get_columns_types()` after every sync, so the next read reflected the table again (DuckDB: ~10 ms, one or two catalog queries elsewhere) | The sync keeps the layout it worked against (reflected before it writes, and again after any `ADD COLUMN` / `ALTER COLUMN` of its own; inserts and updates do not change it) and `Pipe._native_pipe_result` adopts it as `_columns_types` (memory only, with its timestamp) through `_core.SqlConnector.take_synced_columns_types(pipe)`, handed out once. The sync that creates the table hands out nothing (the next read reflects it); a sync that does not finish clears any entry. Row 22's staleness repair is unchanged: `_exists` and `_columns_indices(_timestamp)` are still cleared, and `_columns_types` is never older than the sync that just ended | `Pipe.get_data()` right after a 1% changed DuckDB sync 28.7 / 36.0 ms -> 14.4 / 16.9 ms (the same call on a warm pipe: 18.8 / 19.7), `get_rowcount()` 13.7 / 17.1 -> 1.6 / 2.5 ms, `get_sync_time()` 17.8 / 17.8 -> 3.5 / 3.6 ms (release extension, 1,000 rows, two runs). Tests: `tests/test_pf_perf.py` (no reflection after a sync, added column visible, hard invalidation drops it), `meerschaum-connector-sql/tests/w18_perf.rs::a_sync_hands_out_the_table_layout_once` |
| W18-PF-002 | v4 and rc1 read a pipe's metadata row with two statements (`pipe_id` by keys, then the row by id); a fresh `Pipe` pays them on its first attribute access | `SqlConnector::get_pipe_attributes` reads the row by the pipe's keys in one statement (no observable change: same fields, `{}` for an unregistered or temporary pipe) | a fresh `Pipe(...).get_sync_time()` on TimescaleDB goes from 10 to 6 protocol round trips (proxy count: `Sync` messages per call). Test: `w18_perf.rs::attributes_come_from_one_query` (one `mrsm_pipes` statement, ids and keys equal `get_pipe_id`, with and without a location) |
| W18-PF-003 | rc1 parsed the `get_sync_time` / `get_rowcount` statement on every call on PostgreSQL (unnamed statement: a second round trip) | When the statement text depends only on the table (no `params`; no `begin` / `end` for a row count) it is prepared once per pooled connection, like the catalog queries (`value_catalog`; MySQL already prepared everything, other flavors are unchanged). `params` and bounds keep the unnamed statement so the per-connection statement cache does not fill with literals; a dropped or retyped table is handled by the existing stale-statement retry | warm `get_sync_time()` on TimescaleDB 1.3-1.5 ms -> 0.65-1.2 ms. Test: `w18_perf.rs::sync_time_and_rowcount_follow_the_table` (answers stay right across a drop and a re-creation with another layout) |
| W18-PF-004 | wave 17 candidate "native raw snapshot" (`get_parameters` reads the `_core.Pipe` raw map without the Python getter) | **Not applied.** Measured against the same build: `_core.get_parameters(pipe)` 37.3 us -> 33.0 us and a fresh-`Pipe` `get_sync_time()` 3.17 / 3.33 / 2.95 ms -> 3.11 / 3.24 / 3.14 ms (the difference is inside the noise); the candidate bypasses an overridden `raw_parameters` descriptor on subclasses | benefit below 0.15% of the call against a semantics risk (it would skip a subclass's overridden `raw_parameters`) |

### Wave 18 (task DP): the web console on Python (decisions 27, 29-31, 33-35, 37; design D21)

| ID | v4 / rc1 behavior | v5 handling | Reason / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| W18-DP-001 | v4: one Plotly Dash app (`meerschaum.api.dash`, 7,700 lines) at `/dash`, served by `start api`. rc1: a Rust console, plus one Dash app per plugin at `/dash/<plugin>/` | v4's console restored on the v5 façade, served by the API host at the v4 paths (the Rust server proxies `/dash/*`); the pages, callbacks, assets, components and the `meerschaum.api.dash.*` helper modules are v4.1.4's, with the deleted internals replaced (`api/dash/_support.py`). rc1's per-plugin apps and `make_dash_app()` are gone (`make_dash_app()` / `get_dash_app()` return the one app); `/dash/<plugin>/<rest>` redirects (307) to `/dash/<rest>` | Decisions 27, 29; v4.1.4 vs v5 screenshots of 13 pages differ in < 0.3 % of pixels except the dashboard's right pane (the terminal) and the pipe card (new section); `tests/console`, `tests/api_host` |
| W18-DP-002 | v4 typed the dashboard's action (Exec), a pipe card's Manage menu actions and "Follow logs" into the web terminal beside it over `postMessage` (`termpage.html`) | Exec and Manage run the action through the server (`POST /execute` with a short-lived bearer minted for the session's user), and the output pane follows the job's log over `GET /logs/<job>/stream` (Server-Sent Events, session cookie); "Follow logs" streams the job's own log the same way. Without a server to call (a bare host) the host runs a hidden job (`_dash_exec_<id>`) and polls its log. Delete / drop / clear ask for confirmation first (the job cannot answer a prompt) and run with `--yes`; "Open in Python" still posts to the terminal. The terminal itself is the Rust server's page in an iframe (`/webterm/<session id>`) | Decision 33; `tests/console/test_console.py::test_exec_*`, `test_manage_menu_*`, `test_follow_logs_*`, `tests/api_host/test_console_support.py` |
| W18-DP-003 | `@web_page` functions return Dash layouts | Unchanged, and a function that returns an HTML string (the page contract) is a console page too: the host serves its fragment at `/_pages/<path>` (themed document at `?frame=1`) and the console shows it in an iframe under its own navbar; Plotly figures (`data-mrsm-figure`), maps (`data-mrsm-map`) and HTMX work in the frame (Plotly.js from the `plotly` package, htmx and MapLibre vendored in `meerschaum/api/resources/static/contract/`). Native (Rust) plugin pages come through `GET /_mrsm/native-pages` | Decision 29; `test_page_contract_page_renders_a_figure_in_its_frame`, `test_native_page_renders_the_same_figure` |
| W18-DP-004 | no pipelines page, no Parents/Children on the pipe card | `/dash/pipelines` (dependency graph as a Plotly figure with each pipe's planned action, Gantt of a run, runs table, events) and a "Parents & Children" section on every pipe card, read through the engine bindings (`engine_plan`, `engine_runs`, `engine_run_detail`, `engine_run_events`: the functions behind `/engine/*`). The ledger lag of an edge is the child's planned action and reason (the Rust console read the cursors; the Python bindings do not expose them) | Decision 30; `tests/api_host/test_console_support.py::test_engine_views_*` |
| W18-DP-005 | `set_pipe_query()` (the SQL editor's Update) wrote `fetch: {defintion: ...}` for a pipe that has `fetch:definition` (a typo that lost the edit) | Writes `fetch: {definition: ...}` | v4.1.4 `connectors/sql/_fetch.py` line 424 (not reproduced: it silently dropped the user's edit); `test_pipe_query_helpers` |
| W18-DP-006 | `build_webterm_extra_keys_row` + `[buttons] + [new tab] if TMUX else []`: without tmux the terminal's refresh and fullscreen buttons were missing (operator precedence) | Refresh and fullscreen always show; "+" (new tmux window) only with tmux. The terminal page (`/dash/webterm`) has no fullscreen button (it has no side column) | v4.1.4 `api/dash/webterm.py`; `test_the_key_row_is_hidden_with_room_for_a_keyboard` |
| W18-DP-007 | Dash session lookups hit the cache connector / instance on every callback | Looked up at most once per 5 s per process; dropped when this process sets, updates or deletes the session (a logout is immediate here, at most 5 s late in another process). Unknown sessions are never cached | Decision 34; `test_session_lookups_are_cached_for_a_few_seconds` |
| W18-DP-008 | `dash` extra (v4): `dash`, `dash-bootstrap-components`, `dash-ace`, `dash-extensions`, `dash-daq`, `Flask-Compress`, `terminado`, `tornado`, `a2wsgi` (+ the `api` group) | `meerschaum[dash]` = `dash`, `dash-bootstrap-components`, `dash-ace`, `dash-extensions`, `a2wsgi` + the `api` extra (the host) + `pandas` / `pyarrow` (the pipe cards): `pip install meerschaum[dash]` is a working console. `dash-daq` (unused), `Flask-Compress` (unused), `terminado` and `tornado` (the terminal is Rust) are not needed | Decision 35; `scripts/check_packaging.py`; `test_plugin_dash_app_works_with_the_dash_extra_only` (a bare venv with exactly the extra) |
| W18-DP-009 | `meerschaum.api.dash` built the app when imported | Built on first use (`build_dash_app()` or the first access of `meerschaum.api.dash.dash_app`), so a plugin importing `meerschaum.api.dash.sessions` builds nothing; `console_available()` (a `find_spec` probe) tells whether the packages are there. If they are not, the host mounts nothing, logs one warning and `GET /_mrsm/host/status` says `console.mounted: false` (the Rust server then shows the pointer page) | Decision 32; `test_without_dash_the_console_is_a_pointer_page` |
| W18-DP-010 | rc1: the host served over a Unix socket only | `python -m meerschaum.api.host --tcp HOST:PORT` (mutually exclusive with `--socket`): loopback TCP for Windows; with `MRSM_API_HOST_PROXY_TOKEN` set every HTTP request and websocket handshake needs `X-Mrsm-Host-Proxy-Token` (constant-time compare, 403 / close otherwise) | Decision 36; `test_the_tcp_transport_requires_the_proxy_token` |

| W18-FX4-004 | v4 (SQLAlchemy `pool_pre_ping`) and rc1 (sqlx `test_before_acquire`) ping every connection taken from the pool: one extra round trip per acquire | PostgreSQL and MySQL pools ping a connection only after it sat idle for 1 s or more; a connection used within the last second is trusted (a fresh `Pipe`'s `get_sync_time` makes 8 round trips instead of 12 with its attribute read). A dead connection that was idle longer is still discarded and replaced as before; one the server closed within the last second fails that one query | improvement on v4; `w18_fx4_ping.rs` (PostgreSQL terminated with `pg_terminate_backend`, MySQL `KILL`) |

Every run that leaves pipes `rebuild_pending` says so: a warning listing each pipe, its reason and how to approve (`--yes`, `system.engine.auto_rebuild`) (`--nopretty`: one `rebuild_pending: <pipe>: <reason>` line each on stdout), the summary gains `N not synced: rebuild_pending`, and every `--loop` lap repeats it.
